\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{R_{\ref{Cor_L^2 Noncon_Compare d(M), cN(M) and d(M in Q_R), cN(M in Q_R)}}}{(\tau-T_{\circ})R^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M}\cap Q_{R})-\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{R_{\ref{Cor_L^2 Noncon_Compare d(M), cN(M) and d(M in Q_R), cN(M in Q_R)}}}{(\tau-T_{\circ})R^{2}}\,.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.3
, for every
\tau\in(T_{\circ},T_{\circ}+2]
and
R>0
,

\displaystyle 0\leq 1-\frac{\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M}(\tau)\cap Q_{R})}{\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M}(\tau))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(n)}{(\tau-T_{\circ})R^{2}}\cdot\frac{\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(T_{\circ},\mathcal{M}(T_{\circ}))}{\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M}(\tau))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(n,\epsilon)}{(\tau-T_{\circ})R^{2}}\cdot\frac{\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(T_{\circ}+2,\mathcal{M}(T_{\circ}+2))}{\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M}(\tau))}\leq\frac{C(n,\epsilon)}{(\tau-T_{\circ})R^{2}}

Therefore, when
(\tau-T_{\circ})R^{2}\geq R_{\ref{Cor_L^2 Noncon_Compare d(M), cN(M) and d(M in Q_R), cN(M in Q_R)}}(n,\epsilon)^{2}\gg 1
, we have
\mathcal{M}(\tau)\cap Q_{R}\neq\emptyset
and

\displaystyle\left|e^{\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M}\cap Q_{R})-\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})}-1\right|

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M}(\tau)\cap Q_{R})}{\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M}(\tau))}\cdot\left(\frac{\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau+1,\mathcal{M}(\tau+1)\cap Q_{R})}{\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau+1,\mathcal{M}(\tau+1))}\right)^{-1}-1\right|\leq\frac{C(n,\epsilon)}{(\tau-T_{\circ})R^{2}}\,.

Thus

|\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M}\cap Q_{R})-\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})|\leq\frac{C(n,\epsilon)}{(\tau-T_{\circ})R^{2}}\,.

∎

Let
I\subset\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
. We call a RMCF
I\ni\tau\to\mathcal{M}(\tau)

\delta
-
L^{2}
close to the RMCF
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
on
I
if
\forall\,s\in I
,

(3.19)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{C}_{n,k}]\leq\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{M}(s)]\leq\frac{3}{2}\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{C}_{n,k}]\,,

\displaystyle\mathbf{d}_{n,k}(s,\mathcal{M}(s))\leq\delta\,,

where
\mathcal{F}
denotes the Gaussian area functional. By White’s regularity, if
\mathcal{M}
is
\delta
close to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
in the Brakke sense over
I
, then
\mathcal{M}
is
\Psi(\delta|n,\varepsilon,I)
-
L^{2}
close to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
, and vice versa.

Later, we have various estimates to derive the upper bounds for
\mathbf{d}
in (
3.19
), and hence, to show a flow is
\delta
-
L^{2}
close to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
, the main challenge is to obtain the Gaussian bound. The following lemma implies that whenever
\mathcal{M}(\tau)
is
\delta
-
L^{2}
close to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
for
\tau\in[T-1,T]
, then
\mathcal{M}(\tau)
has the desired Gaussian bound for
\tau\in[T,T+1]
.

Lemma 3.9
.

There exists
\delta_{\ref{lem_delta_L2_close_implies_Gaussian_lower_bound}}(n,\varepsilon)>0
with the following significance. Suppose
\tau\to\mathcal{M}(\tau)
is a rescaled mean curvature flow over
\tau\in[T-1,T+1]
,
\lambda[\mathcal{M}]\leq\varepsilon^{-1}
and
\mathcal{M}
is
\delta_{\ref{lem_delta_L2_close_implies_Gaussian_lower_bound}}
-
L^{2}
close to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
on
[T-1,T]
. Then for
\tau\in[T,T+1]
,

\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{C}_{n,k}]\leq\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{M}(\tau)]\leq\frac{3}{2}\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{C}_{n,k}]\,.

Proof.

By Lemma
C.2
, when
\delta(n,\varepsilon)
is sufficiently small,
\mathcal{M}(T)\cap Q_{R_{n}}
is a
C^{2}
-graph of a function
u
over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{R_{n}}
, with
\|u\|_{C^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{R_{n,\varepsilon}})}\leq C(n,\varepsilon)\delta(n,\varepsilon)
. Then by the Brakke-White regularity theorem,
\mathcal{M}
is a graph of some function over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{R_{n,\varepsilon}/2}
, with
\|u(\cdot,\tau)\|_{C^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{R_{n,\varepsilon}})}\leq C^{\prime}(n,\varepsilon)\delta(n,\varepsilon)
, for
\tau\in[T,T+1]
. Therefore, for
\tau\in[T,T+1]
and a sufficiently large choice of
R_{n,\varepsilon}
, when
\delta\ll 1
, we have
\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{M}(\tau)]\geq\frac{2}{3}\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{R/2}]>\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{C}_{n,k}]
;
\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{M}(\tau)]\leq\frac{4}{3}\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{R_{n}/2}]+O(\varepsilon^{-1}e^{-R^{2}/16})\leq\frac{3}{2}\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{C}_{n,k}]
.
∎

The following Lemma will be used in estimating the graphical radius in Section
4
.

Lemma 3.10
.

For every
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, there exists
\delta_{\ref{Lem_Prelim_M cap Q_R in Q_2n}}(n,\varepsilon)\in(0,\varepsilon)
with the following property.
Let
T\geq 1
,
\tau\mapsto\mathcal{M}(\tau)
be a RMCF in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
with entropy
\lambda[\mathcal{M}]\leq\varepsilon^{-1}
and
\delta_{\ref{Lem_Prelim_M cap Q_R in Q_2n}}
-
L^{2}
close to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
over
[0,T]
. Then for every
\tau\in[1,T]
,

(\spt\mathcal{M})(\tau)\cap\left(B^{n-k+1}_{2n+e^{\tau/2}}\times B^{k}_{2n}\right)\subset Q_{2n}\,.

Proof.

Suppose for contradiction that there exist
\tau_{j}\geq 1
, RMCF
\mathcal{M}_{j}
with
\lambda[\mathcal{M}]\leq\varepsilon^{-1}
which is
1/j
-
L^{2}
close to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
over
[0,\tau_{j}]
, and
(x_{j},y_{j})\in\mathbb{R}^{n-k+1}\times\mathbb{R}^{k}
such that

\displaystyle(x_{j},y_{j})\in(\spt\mathcal{M}_{j})(\tau_{j})\,,

\displaystyle 2n\leq|x_{j}|\leq 2n+e^{\tau_{j}/2},\quad|y_{j}|\leq 2n\,.

Also suppose
|x_{j}|^{-1}
converges to some
a\in[0,1/2n]
.

Consider the time-translated RMCF
\tau\mapsto\hat{\mathcal{M}}_{j}(\tau):=\mathcal{M}_{j}(\tau+\tau_{j}-2\ln|x_{j}|)
. We then see that
\hat{\mathcal{M}}_{j}
is
1/j
-
L^{2}
close to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
over
[2\ln|x_{j}|-\tau_{j},2\ln|x_{j}|]
, and that
(x_{j},y_{j})\in(\spt\hat{\mathcal{M}}_{j})(2\ln|x_{j}|)
.
Let
t\mapsto\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{j}(t):=\sqrt{-t}\,\hat{\mathcal{M}}_{j}(-\ln(-t))
be the MCF associated to
\hat{\mathcal{M}}_{j}
, then when
j\to\infty
, we have
\mathbf{M}_{j}
converges in the Brakke sense to the shrinking round cylinder
t\mapsto\sqrt{-t}\,\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
. However, by the upper semi-continuity of Gaussian density under Brakke convergence, since
(x_{j}/|x_{j}|,y_{j}/|x_{j}|,-|x_{j}|^{-2})\in\spt\hat{\mathbf{M}}_{j}
, its subsequential limit
(\hat{x}_{\infty},0,t_{\infty}=-a^{2})
must be a point in the support of a shrinking round cylinder. This is a contradiction since
|\hat{x}_{\infty}|=1
but
a\leq 1/2n
.
∎

An application of Lemma
3.3
is that the decay order upper bound allows us to take the normalized limit of the graphical function of rescaled mean curvature flow over round cylinders.

Lemma 3.11
.

Let
\beta>0
,
\delta_{j}\searrow 0
; Let
\mathcal{M}_{j}
be a sequence of RMCF in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
over
[0,T]
converging to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
in the Brakke sense, where
T\geq 1
;
\mathbf{u}_{j}\in\mathscr{U}
be a sequence of arrival time functions approaching
\mathbf{U}_{n,k}
as
j\to\infty
. Suppose

\limsup_{j\to\infty}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}_{j}}(0,\mathcal{M}_{j})<+\infty\,.

Let
v_{j}(\cdot,\tau)
be the graphical function of
\mathcal{M}_{j}(\tau)
over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
, defined on a larger and larger domain as
j\to\infty
. Then after passing to a subsequence,
\hat{v}_{j}:=\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}_{j}}(1,\mathcal{M}_{j})^{-1}(v_{j}-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}_{j}})
converges to some non-zero
\hat{v}
in
C^{\infty}_{loc}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\times(0,T])
solving

\partial_{\tau}\hat{v}-L_{n,k}\hat{v}=0\,.

Moreover, for every
\tau\in(0,T]
, we have

\|\hat{v}(\cdot,\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})}=\lim_{j\to\infty}\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}_{j}}(1,\mathcal{M}_{j})^{-1}\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}_{j}}(\tau,\mathcal{M}_{j})<+\infty\,.

We shall call such non-zero renormalized limit
\hat{v}
a
induced (parabolic) Jacobi field
from the sequence
\{\mathcal{M}_{j}\}
relative to
\{\mathbf{u}_{j}\}
. We refer the readers to Lemma
B.5
for properties of parabolic Jacobi fields over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
.

Proof.

By Brakke-White regularity of mean curvature flow and interior parabolic estimate,
v_{j}
is defined on a larger and larger domain exhausting
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
and
(v_{j}-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}_{j}})\to 0
in
C^{\infty}_{loc}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\times(0,T])
as
j\to\infty
.
By Lemma
C.1
, Corollary
3.8
and the upper bound of decay order (denoted by
\epsilon^{-1}
), there exists
R_{1}(n,\epsilon)\gg 1
such that for every
\tau\in(0,T]
, every
R\geq\tau^{-1}R_{1}
and
j\gg 1
, we have

\|(v_{j}-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}_{j}})(\tau,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\setminus Q_{R})}+\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}_{j}}(\tau,\mathcal{M}_{j}\setminus Q_{R})\leq\frac{C(n,\epsilon)}{\tau R^{2}}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}_{j}}(1,\mathcal{M}_{j}\setminus Q_{R}).

Therefore, combined with Lemma
B.5
and the classical parabolic regularity estimates,
\hat{v}_{j}:=\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}_{j}}(1,\mathcal{M}_{j})^{-1}\,(v_{j}-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}_{j}})
subconverges to some non-zero
\hat{v}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\times(0,T])
, and such that for every
\tau\in(0,T]
,

\|\hat{v}(\cdot,\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})}=\lim_{j\to\infty}\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}_{j}}(1,\mathcal{M}_{j})^{-1}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}_{j}}(\tau,\mathcal{M}_{j})<+\infty\,.

∎

Corollary 3.12
.

For every
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2)
, there exists a
\delta_{\ref{Cor_L^2 Mono_RMCF w graphical eigenfunc has cN = spectrum}}(n,\varepsilon)\in(0,\epsilon)
with the following significance.
Let
\mathbf{u}\in\mathscr{U}
,
T_{\circ}>0
so that
\|\varphi_{\mathbf{u}}(\cdot,T_{\circ})\|_{C^{4}}<\delta_{\ref{Cor_L^2 Mono_RMCF w graphical eigenfunc has cN = spectrum}}
,
T\in[1,\varepsilon^{-1}]
,
\mathcal{M}
be a RMCF in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}

\delta_{\ref{Cor_L^2 Mono_RMCF w graphical eigenfunc has cN = spectrum}}
-
L^{2}
close to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
over
[T_{\circ},T_{\circ}+T]
such that

\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(0,\mathcal{M})\leq\epsilon^{-1}\,.

Let
\gamma\in[-\varepsilon^{-1},\varepsilon^{-1}]
,
\tau_{\circ}\in[\varepsilon,T]
,
\sim\in\{\geq,=,\leq\}
, and
v
be the graphical function of
\mathcal{M}
over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
. Also, suppose that

\|\Pi_{\sim\gamma}((v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})(\cdot,\tau_{\circ}))\|_{L^{2}}\geq(1-\delta)\|(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})(\cdot,\tau_{\circ})\|_{L^{2}}\,.

Then for every
\tau\in[\varepsilon,T]
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})-\gamma\begin{cases}\leq\varepsilon,&\text{ if }\sim\text{ is }\leq\,;\\
\geq-\varepsilon,&\text{ if }\sim\text{ is }\geq\,;\\
\in[-\varepsilon,\varepsilon],&\text{ if }\sim\text{ is }=\,.\\
\end{cases}

Proof.

The corollary follows by a direct contradiction argument combining Lemma
3.11
and (ii) of Lemma
B.5
.
∎

Corollary 3.13

(Discrete Monotonicity of the decay order)
.

For every
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2)
, there exists
\delta_{\ref{Cor_L^2 Mono_Discrete Growth Mono}}(n,\varepsilon)\in(0,\varepsilon)
such that the following hold. If
T\geq 0
,
\mathbf{u}\in\mathscr{U}
such that
\|\varphi_{\mathbf{u}}(\cdot,\tau)\|_{C^{4}}<\delta_{\ref{Cor_L^2 Mono_Discrete Growth Mono}}
for
\tau\in[T,T+2]
,
\mathcal{M}
is a RMCF in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}

\delta_{\ref{Cor_L^2 Mono_Discrete Growth Mono}}
-
L^{2}
close to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
over
[T,T+2]
, and satisfies the decay order bound,

(3.20)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(0,\mathcal{M})\leq\epsilon^{-1}\,.

Then at least one of the following holds,

(3.21)

either

\displaystyle-1-\epsilon\leq\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(T+1,\mathcal{M})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(T,\mathcal{M})-\delta_{\ref{Cor_L^2 Mono_Discrete Growth Mono}}\,;

(3.22)

or

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in[T+\epsilon,T+1]}|\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})-\gamma|

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon\,,\;\;\;\;\;\text{ for some }\gamma\in\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})\,.

Moreover, if (
3.21
) fails and
\gamma
is given by (
3.22
), then the graphical function
v
of
\mathcal{M}
over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
satisfies

(3.23)

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{=\gamma}((v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})(\cdot,\tau))\|_{L^{2}}\geq(1-\epsilon)\|(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})(\cdot,\tau)\|_{L^{2}}\,,\quad\forall\,\tau\in[\epsilon,2]\,.

Remark 3.14
.

A useful consequence of at least one of (
3.21
) and (
3.22
) being true is that, if
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
with
\dist_{\mathbb{R}}(\gamma,\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}))\geq\epsilon
, then when
T\geq T_{\circ}
,
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(T,\mathcal{M})\leq\gamma
implies
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(T+1,\mathcal{M})\leq\gamma
.

Proof.

In view of Lemma
3.3
, it suffices to show that for every
\epsilon>0
and sequence of rescaled mean curvature flow
\tau\mapsto\mathcal{M}_{j}(\tau)
over
\tau\in[0,2]
satisfying (
3.20
) with
\|\varphi_{\mathbf{u}_{j}}(\cdot,\tau)\|_{C^{4}}<1/j
for
\tau\in[T,T+2]
and converging to the multiplicity
1
static flow
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
in the Brakke sense as
j\to\infty
, if (
3.21
) fails, namely,

\displaystyle\text{ either }\;\;\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(1,\mathcal{M}_{j})\geq\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}_{j}}(0,\mathcal{M}_{j})-\frac{1}{j}\,,

\displaystyle\text{ or }\;\;\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}_{j}}(1,\mathcal{M}_{j})<-1-\epsilon=\inf\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})-\epsilon\,.

Then (
3.22
) holds, namely, there exists
\gamma\in\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})
such that

\displaystyle\limsup_{j\to\infty}\sup_{\tau\in[T+\epsilon,T+1]}|\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}_{j}}(\tau,\mathcal{M}_{j})-\gamma|=0\,.

and that the induced Jacobi fields from
\{\mathcal{M}_{j}\}
relative to
\{\mathbf{u}_{j}\}
exist and are all given by
e^{-\gamma\tau}w
for some
\gamma
-eigenfunction
w
of
-L_{n,k}
.

By Lemma
3.11
, there always exists an induced Jacobi field
\hat{v}
, and

\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}_{j}}(T,\mathcal{M}_{j})=\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}_{j}}(T+1,\mathcal{M}_{j})=N_{n,k}(v)=:\sigma\,,

where
v\sim e^{-\sigma\tau}
. Here
N_{n,k}(v)
is the linear decay order of a parabolic Jacobi field
v
defined in Lemma
B.5
. Then the desired property is a direct consequence of the parabolic Jacobi field over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
, see Lemma
B.5
.
∎

A direct consequence of Corollary
3.13
is that the decay order at infinity is well-defined for those RMCF that converge to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
.

Corollary 3.15
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a RMCF in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
with finite entropy over
\mathbb{R}_{\tau\geq 0}
such that
\mathcal{M}(\tau)
locally smoothly converges to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
as
\tau\to+\infty
;
\beta\in(0,1)
,
\mathbf{u}\in\mathscr{U}
. Then the following limit exists and belongs to
(\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})\cap\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0})\cup\{+\infty\}
:

(3.24)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\infty,\mathcal{M}):=\lim_{\tau\to+\infty}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})\in(\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})\cap\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0})\cup\{+\infty\}\,.

Proof.

By the
C^{1}
-normal form of the finite entropy RMCF locally smoothly converges to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
(Theorem
6.1
),
\mathcal{M}(\tau)
is
\delta(\tau)
-close to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
, with
\delta(\tau)\to 0
as
\tau\to\infty
. By taking
\varepsilon\to 0
in Corollary
3.13
, we have
\liminf\limits_{\tau\to+\infty}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})\in(\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})\cap\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0})\cup\{+\infty\}
and
\limsup\limits_{\tau\to+\infty}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})\in(\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})\cap\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0})\cup\{+\infty\}
. Moreover, Corollary
3.13
shows that for any
\varepsilon>0
, once
\mathcal{N}(\tau,\mathcal{M})<\lambda-\varepsilon
for
\lambda\in(\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})\cap\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0})
for a sufficiently large
\bar{\tau}
, then
\mathcal{N}(\tau,\mathcal{M})<\lambda-\varepsilon
for all
\tau>\bar{\tau}
. This implies that
\limsup\limits_{\tau\to+\infty}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})=\liminf\limits_{\tau\to+\infty}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})
, which completes the proof.
∎

The following lemma shows that if the rescaled mean curvature flow detects a cylindrical singularity, then the decay order can not be too negative.

Lemma 3.16
.

For every
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, there exists a
\delta_{\ref{Lem_L^2 Mono_cN>-eps if singular}}(n,\varepsilon)>0
with the following properties.
Let
T_{\circ}\geq 0
,
\tau\to\mathcal{M}(\tau)
be a RMCF defined for all
\tau\geq T_{\circ}
, and suppose it is
\delta
-
L^{2}
close to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
on
[T_{\circ},T_{\circ}+1]
with
\delta\leq\delta_{\ref{Lem_L^2 Mono_cN>-eps if singular}}
, but not identical to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
. Suppose

\displaystyle\lambda[\mathcal{M}]\leq\varepsilon^{-1}\,,

\displaystyle\lim_{\tau\to+\infty}\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{M}(\tau)]=\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{C}_{n,k}]\,,

Then we have
\mathcal{N}_{n,k}(\tau,\mathcal{M})\geq-\varepsilon
for every
\tau\geq T_{\circ}+1
.

Proof.

By Brakke-White’s
\epsilon
-regularity
[
Whi05
]
, there exists
R\geq\Psi(\delta|n,\varepsilon)^{-1}
and
v\in C^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{R})
with
\|v\|_{C^{2}}\leq\Psi(\delta|n,\varepsilon)
such that if
\mathcal{M}
is described as in the Lemma with
\delta_{\ref{Lem_L^2 Mono_cN>-eps if singular}}(n,\varepsilon)\ll 1
, then
\mathcal{M}(T_{\circ}+1)
is a
C^{2}
graph over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
in
Q_{R}
with graphical function
v
. In particular,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{M}(T_{\circ}+1)]

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{M}(T_{\circ}+1)\cap Q_{R}}d\mu+\int_{\mathcal{M}(T_{\circ}+1)\setminus Q_{R}}d\mu

\displaystyle\leq(1+C_{n}\|v\|_{C^{2}})\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{R}]+C(n,\varepsilon)e^{-R^{2}/8}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{C}_{n,k}]+\Psi(\delta|n,\varepsilon)\,.

Combining with Huisken’s Monotonicity formula and that
\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{M}(\tau)]\to\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{C}_{n,k}]
as
\tau\to+\infty
, we conclude that for every
\tau\geq T_{\circ}+1
,

|\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{M}(\tau)]-\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{C}_{n,k}]|\leq\Psi(\delta|n,\varepsilon)\,.

By
[
CM25
, Theorem 0.5]
and taking
\delta_{\ref{Lem_L^2 Mono_cN>-eps if singular}}(n,\varepsilon)\ll 1
, we have for every
\tau\geq T_{\circ}+1
,

(3.25)

\displaystyle\mathbf{d}_{n,k}(\tau,\mathcal{M})\leq\delta_{\ref{Cor_L^2 Mono_Discrete Growth Mono}}(n,\varepsilon)\,.

And then
\mathcal{M}
is
\delta_{\ref{Cor_L^2 Mono_Discrete Growth Mono}}
-
L^{2}
close to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
over
\mathbb{R}_{\geq T_{\circ}}
.
By Corollary
3.13
, if
\mathcal{N}_{n,k}(\tau,\mathcal{M})\leq-\varepsilon
for some
\tau\geq T_{\circ}+1
, then inductively
\mathcal{N}_{n,k}(\tau+\ell,\mathcal{M})\leq-\varepsilon
for every integer
\ell\geq 0
. By definition of decay order, this implies,

\mathbf{d}_{n,k}(\tau+\ell,\mathcal{M})\geq\mathbf{d}_{n,k}(\tau,\mathcal{M})e^{\varepsilon\ell}\,.

Since
\mathbf{d}_{n,k}(\tau,\mathcal{M})\neq 0
, this contradicts to (
3.25
) by sending
\ell\to+\infty
.
∎

Corollary 3.17
.

Let
T\geq 0
,
\mathbf{u}\in\mathscr{U}
;
\mathcal{M}
be a RMCF in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
over
[T,T^{\prime}]
with
\lambda[\mathcal{M}]\leq\varepsilon^{-1}
and
\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{M}(T)]>1/2\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{C}_{n,k}]
. Then for every
\tau\in[T,T^{\prime}]
,

\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})\lesssim_{n,\beta,\varepsilon}e^{(1+\varepsilon)(\tau-T)}\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(T,\mathcal{M})\,.

Proof.

Let
[T,\bar{T}]
be the maximum interval such that
\mathcal{M}(\tau)
is
\delta
-
L^{2}
close to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
for
\tau\in[T,\bar{T}]
, where
\delta
is given as in Lemma
3.9
. We consider several situations.

Case 1.
If
\bar{T}
exists and
\bar{T}=T^{\prime}
. Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,
T_{\circ}>0
(only depend on
\mathscr{U}
),
\mathbf{u}\in\mathscr{U}
such that when
\tau>T_{\circ}
,
\|\varphi_{\mathbf{u}}(\cdot,\tau)\|_{C^{4}}\leq\delta_{\ref{Cor_L^2 Mono_Discrete Growth Mono}}
. By Corollary
3.13
, we have
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})\geq-1-\epsilon
for
\tau\in[T_{\circ}+1,T^{\prime}-1]
. In particular, using an induction argument, we can conclude that when
\tau\in[T_{\circ}+1,T^{\prime}-1]
, we have

\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})\leq C(n,\varepsilon)e^{(1+\varepsilon)(\tau-T_{\circ})}\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(T_{\circ},\mathcal{M}).

Again using Lemma
3.3
(over
[T,T_{\circ}]
and
[T^{\prime}-1,T^{\prime}]
) and because
T_{\circ}
only depends on
n,\beta,\varepsilon
, we have for all
\tau\in[T,T^{\prime}]
,

\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})\leq C(n,\varepsilon)e^{(1+\varepsilon)(\tau-T)}\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(T,\mathcal{M}).

Case 2.
If
\bar{T}
exists and
T\leq\bar{T}<T^{\prime}
. Then by the definition of
\delta
-
L^{2}
closeness, and Lemma
3.9
, we must have
\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\bar{T},\mathcal{M})=\delta
. Then we can apply the proof in Case 1 to show that
\delta\leq C(n,\varepsilon)e^{(1+\varepsilon)(\bar{T}-T_{\circ})}\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(T,\mathcal{M})
, and hence for any
\tau\in[\bar{T},T^{\prime}]
,

\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})\leq C(n)\lambda[\mathcal{M}]\leq C(n)\varepsilon^{-1}\leq C(n,\varepsilon)\delta\leq C(n,\varepsilon)e^{(1+\varepsilon)(\tau-T)}\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(T,\mathcal{M}).

Case 3.
If
\bar{T}
does not exist. This implies that either
\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{M}(T)]>3/2\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{C}_{n,k}]
, or
\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(T,\mathcal{M})>\delta
. Note that
\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{M}(T)]>3/2\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{C}_{n,k}]
implies that
\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(T,\mathcal{M})\geq C(n)
from the proof of Lemma
3.9
. Therefore, in the latter two situations, similar to Case 2, we can use Huisken’s monotonicity formula and
\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})\leq C(n)\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{M}(\tau)]
to show that

\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})\leq C(n,\varepsilon)e^{(1+\varepsilon)(\tau-T_{\circ})}\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(T,\mathcal{M}).

∎

4.
Asymptotic profile at cylindrical singular point

The goal of this section is to prove Theorem
1.4
. Recall that the family of low spherical flows
\mathscr{U}
has been defined in Definition
3.5
.
Throughout the section, we assume that
\mathcal{M}:\tau\mapsto\mathcal{M}(\tau)
is a RMCF with finite entropy in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
over
\mathbb{R}_{\tau\geq 0}
, such that
\mathcal{M}(\tau)

C^{\infty}_{loc}
-converges to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
as
\tau\to+\infty
. Let
\mathbf{r}_{1}(\mathcal{M}(\tau))
and
v(\cdot,\tau)
be the
1
-graphical radius and graphical function of
\mathcal{M}(\tau)
over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
.

Case
(i)
of Theorem
1.4
is essentially treated in
[
SX25b
]
. See Section
4.3
.
The bulk of this section is devoted to case
(ii)
and
(iii)
of Theorem
1.4
, i.e., when the flow converges to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
exponentially or even faster. For later applications, we shall prove the following asymptotic profile theorems with a more quantitative estimate. Denote for simplicity the maximal decay order limit, modulo all the low spherical flow to be

\mathcal{N}_{\mathscr{U}}(\infty,\mathcal{M}):=\sup_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathscr{U}}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\infty,\mathcal{M})\ \ (\in\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})\cup\{+\infty\}\,\text{ by Corollary \ref{Cor_L^2 Mono_Def cN_u(infty)}})\,.

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be specified as above, such that
\mathcal{N}_{n,k}(\infty,\mathcal{M})>0
. Then

(4.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\mathscr{U}}(\infty,\mathcal{M})\geq 1/2\,.

Moreover, for every
\varepsilon\in(0,1/4n)
, there exists
\delta_{\ref{Thm_Asymp Profile(New)_gamma>0}}(n,\varepsilon)\in(0,\varepsilon)
with the following significance. If
\mathcal{M}
(as above),
\delta,\gamma,\gamma^{+},\varepsilon
satisfy the following with
\delta\leq\delta_{\ref{Thm_Asymp Profile(New)_gamma>0}}
:

(I)

\mathcal{M}:\tau\mapsto\mathcal{M}(\tau)
is
\delta
-
L^{2}
close to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
over
[0,1]
with
\lambda[\mathcal{M}]\leq\varepsilon^{-1}
;

(II)

\gamma\in\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})\cap[1/2,\varepsilon^{-1}-1]
satisfies

\gamma\leq\mathcal{N}_{\mathscr{U}}(\infty,\mathcal{M})\,.

Let
\gamma^{\prime}:=\min\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})\cap(\gamma,+\infty)
be the minimal spectrum of
-L_{n,k}
greater than
\gamma
; and

\gamma^{+}:=\min\left\{\gamma^{\prime},\ \gamma+\gamma_{n,k},\ 2\gamma\right\}\in(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}]\,,

where recall
\gamma_{n,k}=1/(n-k)
(
\gamma^{+}
will play the role of the decay order for the error term).

Then there exist a unique low spherical flow
\mathbf{u}\in\mathscr{U}
(depending only on
\mathcal{M}
but not on
\gamma
) and a unique eigenfunction
\psi\in\mathrm{W}_{\gamma}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})\cap\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\perp}
of
-L_{n,k}
(non-vanishing if and only if
\gamma=\mathcal{N}_{\mathscr{U}}(\infty,\mathcal{M})
) with
\|\psi\|_{L^{2}}\leq 1
, such that

(4.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\mathscr{U}}(\infty,\mathcal{M})=\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\infty,\mathcal{M})\,;

and that for every
\tau\geq 1
,

(i)

the graphical radius of
\mathcal{M}(\tau)
over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
satisfies,

(4.3)

\displaystyle\mathbf{r}_{1}(\mathcal{M}(\tau))\geq 2n+e^{\frac{\gamma-\varepsilon}{2(\gamma+1)}\cdot\tau}=:\mathrm{R}_{\gamma,\varepsilon}(\tau)\,;

(ii)

for every
2n\leq R\leq\mathrm{R}_{\gamma,\varepsilon}(\tau)
, the graphical function
v(\cdot,\tau)
satisfies a rough
C^{2}
estimate,

(4.4)

\displaystyle\|(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})(\cdot,\tau)\|_{C^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{R})}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-(\gamma-\varepsilon)\tau}R^{2(1+\gamma)}\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(0,\mathcal{M})^{1-\varepsilon}\,;

and an improved error estimate in the asymptotic expansion:

(4.5)

\displaystyle\|(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})(\cdot,\tau)-e^{-\gamma\tau}\psi\|_{C^{0}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{R})}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-(\gamma^{+}-3\varepsilon)\tau}R^{2(2+\gamma^{+})}\,.

Remark 4.2
.

An interesting consequence of Theorem
4.1
follows by taking
\gamma=1/2
, in which case

\gamma^{+}=\frac{1}{2}+\min\{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{n-k}\}=:\gamma_{n,k}^{+}\,.

In fact, for every degenerate
k
-cylindrical singularity
p\in\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})_{+}
, suppose
\mathbf{M}
has tangent flow at
p
to be
\mathcal{C}_{p}\in\operatorname{Rot}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})
. Then the assumptions
(I)
&
(II)
in the Theorem above are satisfied by an appropriate rotation and time translation of the RMCF
\mathcal{M}^{p}
based at
p
, with
\varepsilon:=\min\{\lambda[\mathbf{M}]^{-1},1/4n\}
,
\gamma=1/2
and
\delta=\delta_{\ref{Thm_Asymp Profile(New)_gamma>0}}(n,\varepsilon)
. This determines a low spherical flow
\mathbf{u}_{p}
asymptotic to
\mathcal{C}_{p}
and a unique
\psi_{p}\in\mathrm{W}_{1/2}(\mathcal{C}_{p})
such that the graphical function
v^{p}(\cdot,\tau)
of
\mathcal{M}^{p}(\cdot,\tau)
over
\mathcal{C}_{p}
satisfies

v^{p}(\cdot,\tau)=\varphi_{\mathbf{u}_{p}}(\cdot,\tau)+e^{-\tau/2}\psi_{p}+O(e^{-(\gamma_{n,k}^{+}-3\varepsilon)\tau})\,,\quad\forall\,\tau\gg 1\,.

in
L^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{p})
. And if we denote by
|\mathbf{L}_{p}|:=\operatorname{spine}(\mathcal{C}_{p})
. Then by
(iv)
of Section
2.3.3
,
\psi_{p}
determines a unique
|\mathbf{L}_{p}|^{\perp}
-valued quadratic polynomial
\mathbf{q}_{p}
on
|\mathbf{L}_{p}|
by

\psi_{p}(\theta,y)=:\left\langle\mathbf{q}_{p}(y)-2\tr\mathbf{q}_{p},\hat{\theta}\right\rangle+\mathbf{c}_{p}(y)\,,

where
(\theta,y)\in(|\mathbf{L}_{p}|^{\perp}\cap S^{n}(\sqrt{2(n-k)}))\times|\mathbf{L}_{p}|
parametrizes
\mathcal{C}_{p}
,
\hat{\theta}:=\theta/|\theta|
;
\mathbf{c}_{p}
is a cubic Hermitian polynomial on
|\mathbf{L}_{p}|\approx\mathbb{R}^{k}
.

4.1.
Asymptotic profile relative to a fixed spherical flow

We first treat the case relative to a fixed low spherical flow
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{u}}
. The following Lemma provides an almost optimal graphical radius estimate, together with a rough
C^{2}
estimate in spacetime. We emphasize that we don’t need to assume a decay order upper bound in this Lemma.

Lemma 4.3
.

\forall\,\varepsilon\in(0,1/(4n))
, there exists
\delta_{\ref{Lem_AsympProf_Rough C^2 est}}(n,\varepsilon)\in(0,\varepsilon)

with the following significance. Suppose

(a)

\mathcal{M}
is a RMCF
\delta
-
L^{2}
close to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
over
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
, with
\delta\leq\delta_{\ref{Lem_AsympProf_Rough C^2 est}}
and entropy bound
\lambda[\mathcal{M}]\leq\varepsilon^{-1}
;

(b)

\mathbf{u}\in\mathscr{U}
is a low spherical flow with
\|\varphi_{\mathbf{u}}\|_{C^{4}}\leq\delta_{\ref{Lem_AsympProf_Rough C^2 est}}
;
\mu\in(2\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{-1}]
such that
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\infty,\mathcal{M})\geq\mu
.

Then for every
\tau\geq 1
, we have graphical radius lower bound,

(4.6)

\displaystyle\mathbf{r}_{1}(\mathcal{M}(\tau))\geq 2n+e^{\frac{\mu-\varepsilon}{2(\mu+1)}\cdot\tau}=:\mathrm{R}_{\mu,\varepsilon}(\tau)\,;

and a rough
C^{2}
estimate of graphical function
v(\cdot,\tau)
of
\mathcal{M}(\tau)
over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
:
\forall\,2n\leq R\leq\mathrm{R}_{\mu,\varepsilon}(\tau)
,

(4.7)

\displaystyle\|(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})(\cdot,\tau)\|_{C^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{R})}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-(\mu-\varepsilon)\tau}R^{2(1+\mu)}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(0,\mathcal{M})^{1-\varepsilon}\,.

Proof.

We first claim that by taking
\delta_{\ref{Lem_AsympProf_Rough C^2 est}}(n,\varepsilon)\ll 1
and
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(n,\varepsilon)\gg 1
, we have for every
\tau\geq\mathrm{T}^{\prime}
,

(4.8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})\geq\mu-\varepsilon/2\,.

Note that this implies that (by definition) for every
\tau\geq\mathrm{T}^{\prime}
,

(4.9)

\displaystyle\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})\leq e^{-(\mu-\varepsilon/2)\lfloor\tau-\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\rfloor}\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau-\lfloor\tau-\mathrm{T}^{\prime}\rfloor,\mathcal{M})\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-(\mu-\varepsilon/2)\tau}\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(0,\mathcal{M})\,;

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
3.3
; while since we have fix the choice of
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(n,\varepsilon)
here, also by Lemma
3.3
, the same estimate holds for
\tau\in[0,\mathrm{T}^{\prime}]
.

To prove (
4.8
), first note that there exist an
\varepsilon^{\prime}(n,\varepsilon)\in(0,\varepsilon)
and
\hat{\mu}\in[\mu-\varepsilon/2,\mu-\varepsilon/4]
such that
\dist_{\mathbb{R}}(\hat{\mu},\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}))\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}
. Let
\delta_{\ref{Lem_AsympProf_Rough C^2 est}}(n,\varepsilon)\ll 1
and
\mathrm{T}^{\prime}(n,\varepsilon)\gg 1
such that when
\tau\geq\mathrm{T}^{\prime}
,
\|\varphi_{\mathbf{u}}(\cdot,\tau)\|_{C^{4}}
is sufficiently small so that Corollary
3.13
can be applied. Suppose for contradiction, there exists
\bar{\tau}\geq\mathrm{T}^{\prime}
such that
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\bar{\tau},\mathcal{M})<\mu-\varepsilon/2\leq\hat{\mu}
. Then by Corollary
3.13
, for every integer
l\geq 0
,

\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\bar{\tau}+l,\mathcal{M})\leq\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\bar{\tau},\mathcal{M})\leq\hat{\mu}\leq\mu-\varepsilon/4\,.

This contradicts to assumption
(b)
by sending
l\to+\infty
.

Now we bound the
L^{2}
distance of the translated flow. For every
\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
and
\tau\geq 0
, we denote for simplicity
\mathbf{y}_{\tau}:=e^{\tau/2}\mathbf{y}
, then recall that
\tau\mapsto\mathcal{M}^{(0,\mathbf{y},0)}(\tau)=\mathcal{M}(\tau)-(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{y}_{\tau})
is another RMCF. Hence whenever
|\mathbf{y}|\leq e^{-\tau/2}
, by applying (
3.15
) of Lemma
3.7
with
R=8(-\ln\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M}))^{1/2}
,

(4.10)

\displaystyle\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M}^{(0,\mathbf{y},0)})=\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M}(\tau)-(0,\mathbf{y}_{\tau}))\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})^{1-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}\,,

where
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}:=1-(\mu-\varepsilon)/(\mu-\varepsilon/2)\leq\varepsilon
has a positive lower bound depending only on
\varepsilon
.

Now that for every
\tau_{\circ}\geq 0
, every
\tau\geq\tau_{\circ}+1
and
\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
with
|\mathbf{y}|\leq e^{-\tau_{\circ}/2}
, whenever the terms on the RHS are sufficiently small, we have

(4.11)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau-1,\mathcal{M}^{(0,\mathbf{y},0)})&\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{(1+\varepsilon)(\tau-\tau_{\circ})}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau_{\circ},\mathcal{M}^{(0,\mathbf{y},0)})\\
&\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{(1+\varepsilon)(\tau-\tau_{\circ})}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau_{\circ},\mathcal{M})^{1-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}\\
&\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{(1+\varepsilon)(\tau-\tau_{\circ})}\cdot\left(e^{-(\mu-\varepsilon/2)\tau_{\circ}}\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(0,\mathcal{M})\right)^{1-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}\\
&\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{(1+\varepsilon)\tau-(1+\mu)\tau_{\circ}}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(0,\mathcal{M})^{1-\varepsilon}\,,\end{split}

where the first inequality follows from Corollary
3.17
and Lemma
3.9
, the second follows from (
4.10
) and the third follows from (
4.8
).
In particular, if we take
\breve{\tau}:=(\mu+1)^{-1}\cdot(1+\varepsilon)\tau
, then for every
\tau_{\circ}\in[\breve{\tau},\tau-1]
and every
|\mathbf{y}|\leq e^{-\tau_{\circ}/2}
, the inequality above gives,

\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau-1,\mathcal{M}^{(0,\mathbf{y},0)})\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(0,\mathcal{M})^{1-\varepsilon}\,.

By Lemma
3.9
, Lemma
C.2
and taking
\delta_{\ref{Lem_AsympProf_Rough C^2 est}}(n,\varepsilon)\ll 1
, this means
\mathcal{M}^{(0,\mathbf{y},0)}(\tau)=\mathcal{M}(\tau)-e^{\tau/2}\mathbf{y}
is
C^{2}
graphical over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
in
Q_{2n}
, and hence
\mathcal{M}(\tau)
is a
C^{2}
graph over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
in
Q_{2n}(0,e^{\tau/2}\mathbf{y})
, with the graphical function
v(\cdot,\tau)
satisfying

\|(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})(\cdot,\tau)\|_{C^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{2n}(0,e^{\tau/2}\mathbf{y}))}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{(1+\varepsilon)\tau-(1+\mu)\tau_{\circ}}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(0,\mathcal{M})^{1-\varepsilon}\,.

In view of the choice of admissible
\mathbf{y}
, this proves that for every
\tau\geq 1
, every
2n\leq R\leq 2n+e^{(\tau-\breve{\tau})/2}=:\mathrm{R}_{\mu,\varepsilon}(\tau)
,
\mathcal{M}(\tau)
is a
C^{2}
graph over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
in
B^{n-k+1}_{2n}\times B^{k}_{R}
, with estimate on graphical function
v(\cdot,\tau)
:

\displaystyle\|(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})(\cdot,\tau)\|_{C^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{R})}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-(\mu-\varepsilon)\tau}R^{2(1+\mu)}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(0,\mathcal{M})^{1-\varepsilon}\,.

To finish the proof of (
4.6
) and (
4.7
), it suffices to establish the following:

(4.12)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(\tau)\cap Q_{\mathrm{R}_{\mu,\varepsilon}(\tau)}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{2n}^{n-k+1}\times\mathbb{R}^{k}=\emptyset\,.

To see this, for every
T>1
, note that by applying (
4.11
) again with
\tau_{\circ}=\breve{T}:=(1+\mu)^{-1}\cdot(1+\varepsilon)T
, we have for every
|\mathbf{y}|\leq e^{-\breve{T}/2}
and every
\tau\in[\breve{T},T]
,

\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M}^{(0,\mathbf{y},0)})\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{(1+\varepsilon)\tau-(1+\mu)\breve{T}}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(0,\mathcal{M})^{1-\varepsilon}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(0,\mathcal{M})^{1-\varepsilon}\,;

and the same holds when
0\leq\tau\leq\breve{T}
by Lemma
3.7
since
|e^{\tau/2}\mathbf{y}|\leq 1
for such
\tau
. By taking
\delta_{\ref{Lem_AsympProf_Rough C^2 est}}(n,\varepsilon)\ll 1
, this implies that
\mathcal{M}^{(0,\mathbf{y},0)}
is
\delta_{\ref{Lem_Prelim_M cap Q_R in Q_2n}}(n,\varepsilon)
-close to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
over
[0,T]
. Thus by Lemma
3.10
,

(\spt\mathcal{M}^{(0,\mathbf{y},0)})(T)\cap\left(B^{n-k+1}_{2n+e^{\tau/2}}\times B^{k}_{2n}\right)\subset Q_{2n}.

By ranging
\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
with constraint
|\mathbf{y}|\leq e^{-\breve{T}/2}
, this proves (
4.12
).
∎

We now turn the rough estimate (
4.7
) to one with a precise time asymptotic. This is achieved by solving an inhomogeneous parabolic equation with a pointwise estimate, which we discuss in the Appendix.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
\varepsilon,\delta\leq\delta_{\ref{Lem_AsympProf_Rough C^2 est}},\mathcal{M},\mathbf{u},\mu
be the same as in Lemma
4.3
. Suppose further that

(4.13)

\displaystyle\lim_{\tau\to+\infty}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})=\mu\,,

\displaystyle\mu^{+}:=\min\left\{\mu^{\prime},\ \mu+\gamma_{n,k},\ 2\mu\right\}>\mu+4\varepsilon\,.

where
\mu^{\prime}:=\min\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})\cap(\mu,+\infty)
.
Then
\mu\in\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})
, and there’s a unique nonzero eigenfunction
\psi\in\mathrm{W}_{\mu}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})
of
-L_{n,k}
such that the following refined estimate of graphical function
v(\cdot,\tau)
of
\mathcal{M}(\tau)
over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
holds: for every
\tau\geq 1
and
2n\leq R\leq\mathbf{r}_{1}(\mathcal{M}(\tau))
,

(4.14)

\displaystyle\|(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})(\cdot,\tau)-e^{-\mu\tau}\psi\|_{C^{0}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{R})}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-(\mu^{+}-3\varepsilon)\tau}R^{2(2+\mu^{+})}\,.

In particular,
\exists\,\delta_{\ref{Lem_AsympProf_Precise C^0 est}}(n,\varepsilon)\in(0,\delta_{\ref{Lem_AsympProf_Rough C^2 est}}]
such that if
\mathcal{M}
further satisfies
\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(0,\mathcal{M})\leq\delta_{\ref{Lem_AsympProf_Precise C^0 est}}
, then

(4.15)

\displaystyle\|\psi\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(0,\mathcal{M})^{(1-\varepsilon)/2}\leq 1\,;

and for every
\tau\geq 1
,

(4.16)

\displaystyle\|(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})(\cdot,\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{\mathbf{r}_{1}(\mathcal{M}(\tau))})}+\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-\mu\tau}\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(0,\mathcal{M})^{\delta_{\ref{Lem_AsympProf_Precise C^0 est}}}\,.

Remark 4.5
.

By applying the RMCF equation to
v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}}
, one can also derive a
C^{k,\alpha}
bound on
(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})(\cdot,\tau)-e^{-\mu\tau}\psi
of similar form as (
4.14
). But this is out of the scope of this paper.

Proof of Lemma
4.4
.

Recall
\mathrm{R}_{\mu,\varepsilon}(\tau):=2n+e^{\frac{\mu-\varepsilon}{2(\mu+1)}\cdot\tau}
denotes the lower bound on graphical radius as in Lemma
4.3
.
Also note that there exist
\varepsilon^{\prime}\in(0,\varepsilon)
depending on
\mu
,
\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}=\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}(n,\varepsilon)\in(0,\varepsilon)
, and
\hat{\mu}\in[\mu^{+}-3\varepsilon,\mu^{+}-2\varepsilon]
such that

(4.17)

\displaystyle\dist_{\mathbb{R}}(\hat{\mu},\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}))\geq\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}\,,

\displaystyle[\mu-2\varepsilon^{\prime},\mu+2\varepsilon^{\prime}]\cap\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})\subset\{\mu\}\,.

Let
\xi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
be a cut-off function with
\xi|_{(-\infty,1/2]}=1
,
\xi|_{[1,+\infty)}=0
and
|\xi^{\prime}|,|\xi^{\prime\prime}|\leq 2025
; let

\tilde{\xi}(\theta,y,\tau):=\xi(\mathrm{R}_{\mu,\varepsilon}(\tau)^{-1}|y|),\qquad\tilde{v}(\cdot,\tau):=(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})(\cdot,1+\tau)\tilde{\xi}\,.

Note that by (
4.13
),
|\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})-\mu|<\varepsilon^{\prime}
for
\tau\gg 1
, hence by Lemma
C.1

(iii)
and the exponential growth of
\mathrm{R}_{\mu,\varepsilon}(\tau)
, we have

(4.18)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\limsup_{\tau\to+\infty}e^{(\mu-\varepsilon^{\prime})\tau}\|\tilde{v}(\cdot,\tau)\|_{L^{2}}<+\infty\,;\\
&\liminf_{\tau\to+\infty}\ e^{(\mu+\varepsilon^{\prime})\tau}\|\tilde{v}(\cdot,\tau)\|_{L^{2}}\geq\liminf_{\tau\to+\infty}\ e^{(\mu+\varepsilon^{\prime})\tau}\left(\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})-\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M}(\tau)\setminus Q_{\mathrm{R}_{\mu,\varepsilon}(\tau)/2}))\right)>0\,.\end{split}

Claim
.

\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}:=(\partial_{\tau}-L_{n,k})\tilde{v}
satisfies the pointwise estimate,

(4.19)

\displaystyle|\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}(\cdot,\tau)|\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-\hat{\mu}\tau}(1+|y|^{2})^{\hat{\mu}+2+2\varepsilon}\,.

Proof of the Claim.

We denote for simplicity
|J^{2}v|:=|v|+|\nabla v|+|\nabla^{2}v|
. By
[
SWX25
, Lemma B.2]
,

\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}=\tilde{\xi}\cdot(\partial_{\tau}-L_{n,k})(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})+(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})\cdot(\partial_{\tau}-L_{n,k}+1)\tilde{\xi}-2\nabla\tilde{\xi}\cdot\nabla(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})

satisfies the pointwise estimate (we use
\mathds{1}_{E}
to denote the indicator function of
E
),

\displaystyle|\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}(\cdot,\tau)|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n}\Big(|J^{2}(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})(\cdot,\tau)|+|J^{2}\varphi_{\mathbf{u}}(\cdot,\tau)|+\mathds{1}_{\{1/2\leq|y|/\mathrm{R}_{\mu,\varepsilon}(\tau)\leq 1\}}\Big)|J^{2}(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})(\cdot,\tau)|\cdot\mathds{1}_{Q_{\mathrm{R}_{\mu,\varepsilon}(\tau)}}

To derive (
4.19
), we shall bound each term on the RHS above. By the definition of
\mathrm{R}_{\tau}
,

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{\{\mathrm{R}_{\mu,\varepsilon}(\tau)/2\leq|y|\leq\mathrm{R}_{\mu,\varepsilon}(\tau)\}}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-(\mu-\varepsilon)\tau}\cdot(1+|y|^{2})^{\mu+1}\mathds{1}_{Q_{\mathrm{R}_{\mu,\varepsilon}(\tau)}}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}1\,;

Since
\mathbf{u}\in\mathscr{U}
, by item
(3)
of Lemma
3.4
,

\displaystyle|J^{2}\varphi_{\mathbf{u}}(\cdot,\tau)|\lesssim_{n}e^{-\gamma_{n,k}\tau}\,;

while by Lemma
4.3
,

\displaystyle|J^{2}(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})(\cdot,\tau)|\cdot\mathds{1}_{Q_{\mathrm{R}_{\mu,\varepsilon}(\tau)}}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-(\mu-\varepsilon)\tau}(1+|y|^{2})^{\mu+1}\mathds{1}_{Q_{\mathrm{R}_{\mu,\varepsilon}(\tau)}}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}1\,.

Also by our choice of
\hat{\mu}
, it’s easy to check that
\frac{\hat{\mu}}{\mu-\varepsilon}\leq 2
and
\frac{(\mu+1)\hat{\mu}}{\mu-\varepsilon}\leq\hat{\mu}+2+2\varepsilon
. Hence

\displaystyle\left(e^{-(\mu-\varepsilon)\tau}(1+|y|^{2})^{\mu+1}\cdot\mathds{1}_{Q_{\mathrm{R}_{\mu,\varepsilon}(\tau)}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}\left(e^{-(\mu-\varepsilon)\tau}(1+|y|^{2})^{\mu+1}\cdot\mathds{1}_{Q_{\mathrm{R}_{\mu,\varepsilon}(\tau)}}\right)^{\frac{\hat{\mu}}{\mu-\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-\hat{\mu}\tau}(1+|y|^{2})^{\hat{\mu}+2+2\varepsilon}\,;

\displaystyle e^{-\gamma_{n,k}\tau}\cdot\left(e^{-(\mu-\varepsilon)\tau}(1+|y|^{2})^{\mu+1}\cdot\mathds{1}_{Q_{\mathrm{R}_{\mu,\varepsilon}(\tau)}}\right)\leq e^{-\hat{\mu}\tau}(1+|y|^{2})^{\hat{\mu}+2+2\varepsilon}\,.

Combining the estimates above finishes the proof of pointwise estimate (
4.19
).
∎

Now recall by our assumption (
4.17
),
\hat{\mu}\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}}(\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}))
. Hence by (
4.19
) and Proposition
B.1
, there’s a solution to

(\partial_{\tau}-L_{n,k})w=\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}

with the following pointwise spacetime
C^{0}
estimate,

(4.20)

\displaystyle|w(\cdot,\tau)|\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-\hat{\mu}\tau}(1+|y|^{2})^{\hat{\mu}+2+2\varepsilon}\,.

Then notice that
(\partial_{\tau}-L_{n,k})(\tilde{v}-w)=0
, and combining (
4.20
) with (
4.18
), we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{\tau\to+\infty}e^{(\mu-\varepsilon^{\prime})\tau}\|(\tilde{v}-w)(\cdot,\tau)\|_{L^{2}}<+\infty\,,

\displaystyle\liminf_{\tau\to+\infty}e^{(\mu+\varepsilon^{\prime})\tau}\|(\tilde{v}-w)(\cdot,\tau)\|_{L^{2}}>0\,.

While by equation (
4.7
) of Lemma
4.3
and (
4.20
), the following pointwise estimate hold at
\tau=0
:

\displaystyle|(\tilde{v}-w)(\cdot,0)|\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}(1+|y|^{2})^{\hat{\mu}+2+2\varepsilon}\,.

Recall again by our assumption (
4.17
),
[\mu-\varepsilon^{\prime},\mu+\varepsilon^{\prime}]\cap\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})\subset\{\mu\}
,
\mu+\varepsilon\leq\hat{\mu}\leq\mu^{\prime}-\varepsilon
. Then by applying Proposition
B.2
with
\lambda=\hat{\mu},\lambda^{\prime}:=\hat{\mu}+2+2\varepsilon
we get,
\mu\in\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})
,
\psi:=\Pi_{=\mu}((\tilde{v}-w)(\cdot,0))
is non-zero and the following pointwise bound holds,

\displaystyle|(\tilde{v}-w)(\cdot,\tau)-e^{-\mu\tau}\psi|\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-\hat{\mu}\tau}(1+|y|^{2})^{\hat{\mu}+2+2\varepsilon}\leq e^{-(\mu^{+}-3\varepsilon)\tau}(1+|y|^{2})^{2+\mu^{+}}\,.

Combining this with (
4.20
) proves (
4.14
) for
2n\leq R\leq\mathrm{R}_{\mu,\varepsilon}(\tau)
.
While if
\mathrm{R}_{\mu,\varepsilon}(\tau)\leq R\leq\mathbf{r}_{1}(\mathcal{M}(\tau))
, we have (by definition of graphical radius),

\displaystyle\|(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})(\cdot,\tau)-e^{-\mu\tau}\psi\|_{C^{0}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{R})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}\|(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})(\cdot,\tau)\|_{C^{0}(Q_{R})}+e^{-\mu\tau}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}(Q_{R})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}1+e^{-\mu\tau}R^{2\mu+2}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-(\mu^{+}-3\varepsilon)\tau}R^{2(2+\mu^{+})}.

This proves (
4.14
) for such
R
.

Finally, by combining (
4.7
) and (
4.14
), for every
\tau\geq 1
we have

\|\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{\mathrm{R}_{\mu,\varepsilon}(\tau)})}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-(\mu^{+}-\mu-3\varepsilon)\tau}+e^{\varepsilon\tau}\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(0,\mathcal{M})^{1-\varepsilon}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-\varepsilon\tau}+e^{\varepsilon\tau}\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(0,\mathcal{M})^{1-\varepsilon}.

In particular, when
\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(0,\mathcal{M})\leq\delta_{\ref{Lem_AsympProf_Precise C^0 est}}(n,\varepsilon)\ll 1
, by applying Corollary
3.6
with
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}^{\prime}
and
w=\psi
, taking
\tau:=\frac{\varepsilon-1}{2\varepsilon}\cdot\ln\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(0,\mathcal{M})
yields estimate (
4.15
) on
\|\psi\|_{L^{2}}
.

To prove (
4.16
), first note that by item
(iii)
of Lemma
C.1
and Lemma
3.3
,

\displaystyle\|(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})(\cdot,\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{\mathbf{r}_{1}(\mathcal{M}(\tau))})}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-\mu\tau}e^{(K_{n}+\mu)\tau}\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(0,\mathcal{M}).

This implies (
4.16
) if
\tau\geq 1
such that
e^{(K_{n}+\varepsilon^{-1})\tau}\leq\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(0,\mathcal{M})^{-1/2}
.

While if
e^{(K_{n}+\varepsilon^{-1})\tau}\geq\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(0,\mathcal{M})^{-1/2}
, the estimate (
4.15
) with (
4.6
) on
\psi
, estimate (
4.14
) on
|v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}}-e^{-\mu\tau}\psi|
and item
(iii)
of Lemma
C.1
together yields,

\displaystyle\|(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})(\cdot,\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{\mathbf{r}_{1}(\mathcal{M}(\tau))})}+\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-\mu\tau}\|\psi\|_{L^{2}}+e^{-(\mu^{+}-3\varepsilon)\tau}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-\mu\tau}\left(\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(0,\mathcal{M})^{(1-\varepsilon)/2}+e^{-\varepsilon\tau}\right)\,,

which proves (
4.16
) if
\delta_{\ref{Lem_AsympProf_Precise C^0 est}}
is chosen smaller.
∎

4.2.
Find optimal low spherical flow

We shall prove Theorem
4.1
in this subsection by finding the low spherical flow that best approximates our RMCF.

Proof of Theorem
4.1
.

When
n-k=1
, by definition, the only low spherical flow is
\mathbf{U}_{n,k}
. Hence (
4.1
) follows from Corollary (
3.15
); (
4.2
) is vacuum; (
4.3
), (
4.4
) and (
4.5
) follow from Lemmas
4.3
and
4.4
. From now on, let’s assume
n-k\geq 2
.
Recall that
\mathbf{r}_{1}(\mathcal{M}(\tau))
and
v(\cdot,\tau)
denote the
1
-graphical radius and graphical function of
\mathcal{M}(\tau)
over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
. We may also assume without loss of generality that
\lambda[\mathcal{M}]\leq\varepsilon^{-1}
.

Let
\gamma_{0}:=\mathcal{N}_{n,k}(\infty,\mathcal{M})>0
. Note that by Corollary
3.15
,
\gamma_{0}\in\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})
, hence
\gamma_{0}\geq\gamma_{n,k}
.
When
\gamma_{0}=\gamma_{n,k}
, by taking
\delta\leq\min\{\delta_{\ref{Lem_AsympProf_Rough C^2 est}},\delta_{\ref{Lem_AsympProf_Precise C^0 est}}\}
and applying Lemma
4.3
(
4.6
) and Lemma
4.4
(
4.14
) to
\mathcal{M}
with
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{U}_{n,k}
, there’s a unique nonzero eigenfunction
\psi_{0}\in\mathrm{W}_{\gamma_{n,k}}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})
such that for every
\tau\gg 1
,

(4.21)

\displaystyle\|v(\cdot,\tau)-e^{-\gamma_{n,k}\tau}\psi_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-(2\gamma_{n,k}-3\varepsilon)\tau}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbf{r}_{1}(\mathcal{M}(\tau))\geq 2n+e^{\frac{\gamma_{n,k}-\varepsilon}{1+\gamma_{n,k}}\cdot\tau}\,.

By Lemma
4.3
(
4.7
), this is still true if
\gamma_{0}>\gamma_{n,k}
, with
\psi_{0}=0
.
Let
\psi_{\mathbb{S}}\in\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{S}}
(possibly
0
) be the
L^{2}
-orthogonal projection of
\psi_{0}
onto
\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{S}}
. Then when
\psi_{\mathbb{S}}\neq 0
, we must have
\gamma=\gamma_{n,k}
and by Lemma
4.4
(
4.15
), we have

\|\psi_{\mathbb{S}}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|\psi_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}\mathbf{d}_{n,k}(0,\mathcal{M})^{(1-\varepsilon)/2}\,.

Hence by taking
\delta_{\ref{Thm_Asymp Profile(New)_gamma>0}}(n,\varepsilon)\ll 1
, we can apply Lemma
3.4
and find a unique low spherical flow
\mathbf{u}\in\mathscr{U}
such that

(4.22)

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{\mathbf{u}}(\cdot,\tau)-e^{-\gamma_{n,k}\tau}\psi_{\mathbb{S}}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{n}e^{-\frac{3}{2}\gamma_{n,k}\tau}\,.

Claim.
For every low spherical flow
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{U}\setminus\{\mathbf{u}\}
, we have

\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}}(\infty,\mathcal{M})=\gamma_{n,k}\leq\frac{1}{2}\leq\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\infty,\mathcal{M})\,.

We first finish the proof of Theorem
4.1
assuming this Claim. (
4.1
) and (
4.2
) follow directly from this Claim. Moreover, let
\varepsilon,\mathcal{M},\delta,\gamma,\gamma^{+}
be specified in assumption
(I)
,
(II)
of Theorem
4.1
, and
\mathbf{u}\in\mathscr{U}
be determined as above. Then by taking
\delta_{\ref{Thm_Asymp Profile(New)_gamma>0}}(n,\varepsilon)\ll 1
, the estimate (
4.3
) on graphical radius and (
4.4
) on
C^{2}
norm of graphical function follows from Lemma
4.3
with
\gamma
in place of
\mu
.

To prove the improved error estimate (
4.5
), first note that if
\gamma<\mathcal{N}_{\mathscr{U}}(\infty,\mathcal{M})
, then by definition of
\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{+}
in
(II)
and the fact that
\mathcal{N}_{\mathscr{U}}(\infty,\mathcal{M})\in\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})\cup\{+\infty\}
, we have

\gamma^{+}\leq\gamma^{\prime}\leq\mathcal{N}_{\mathscr{U}}(\infty,\mathcal{M})\,.

Hence, we can again apply Lemma
4.3
(
4.7
) with
\gamma^{+}
in place of
\mu
. This proves (
4.5
) with
\psi=0
.

Finally, if
\gamma=\mathcal{N}_{\mathscr{U}}(\infty,\mathcal{M})=\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\infty,\mathcal{M})
, then by taking
\delta\leq\min\{\delta_{\ref{Lem_AsympProf_Rough C^2 est}}(n,\varepsilon),\delta_{\ref{Lem_AsympProf_Precise C^0 est}}(n,\varepsilon)^{10}\}
with
\gamma
in place of
\mu
and applying (
4.14
) of Lemma
4.4
, we derive that there exists a unique nonzero eigenfunction
\psi\in\mathrm{W}_{\gamma}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})
with
\|\psi\|_{L^{2}}\leq 1
such that estimate (
4.5
) holds (with
\gamma^{+}
in place of
\mu^{+}
). Moreover, together with the graphical radius estimate, this implies

(4.23)

\displaystyle\|(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})(\cdot,\tau)-e^{-\gamma\tau}\psi\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-(\gamma^{+}-3\varepsilon)\tau}\,.

We are left to show that

(4.24)

\displaystyle\psi\perp\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{S}}\,.

This is obvious when
\gamma\neq\gamma_{n,k}
since different eigenspaces are perpendicular to each other. When
\gamma=\gamma_{n,k}
, we need slightly more effort. First note that by the Claim,
\gamma_{n,k}\leq\gamma_{0}\leq 1/2\leq\gamma
, which then forces
\gamma_{n,k}=\gamma_{0}=\gamma
and
n-k=2
. While by combining (
4.21
), (
4.22
) and (
4.23
), we have

\displaystyle e^{-\gamma\tau}\|\psi_{0}-\psi_{\mathbb{S}}-\psi\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\|v(\cdot,\tau)-e^{-\gamma_{n,k}\tau}\psi_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\varphi_{\mathbf{u}}(\cdot,\tau)-e^{-\gamma_{n,k}\tau}\psi_{\mathbb{S}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})(\cdot,\tau)-e^{-\gamma\tau}\psi\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-(2\gamma_{n,k}^{+}-3\varepsilon)\tau}+e^{-\frac{3}{2}\gamma_{n,k}\tau}+e^{-(\gamma^{+}-3\varepsilon)\tau}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-(\gamma+\varepsilon)\tau}\,,

where in the last inequality, we use the fact that
\varepsilon\in(0,1/4n)
. Sending
\tau\to+\infty
, this shows
\psi_{0}=\psi_{\mathbb{S}}+\psi
. Recall that
\psi_{\mathbb{S}}
is the orthogonal projection of
\psi_{0}
onto
\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{S}}
, we thus conclude (
4.24
) and finish the proof when
\mathcal{N}_{n,k}(\infty,\mathcal{M})<+\infty
.

Proof of Claim.

For every
\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{U}
corresponding to
\psi^{\prime}\neq\psi_{\mathbb{S}}
as in Lemma
3.4
, by the graphical estimate (
4.21
) and the cut-off estimate Lemma
2.6
,
\mathbf{r}_{1}(\mathcal{M}(\tau))\geq\tau
when
\tau\gg 1
. Hence,

\displaystyle\left|\|(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}})(\cdot,\tau)\|_{L^{2}(Q_{\tau})}-\|e^{-\gamma_{n,k}\tau}\psi_{0}-e^{-\gamma_{n,k}\tau}\psi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim_{n}\|v(\cdot,\tau)-e^{-\gamma_{n,k}\tau}\psi_{0}\|_{L^{2}(Q_{\tau})}+\|\varphi_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}}(\cdot,\tau)-e^{-\gamma_{n,k}\tau}\psi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}+e^{-\tau^{2}/16}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-(2\gamma_{n,k}-3\varepsilon)\tau}+e^{-\frac{3}{2}\gamma_{n,k}\tau}+e^{-\tau^{2}/16}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-(\gamma_{n,k}+\varepsilon)\tau}\,.

Here the second inequality follows from (
4.21
) and (
4.22
).

While by combining with Lemma
C.1

(iii)
, when
\tau\gg 1
, we derive

\displaystyle\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))\|(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}})(\cdot,\tau)\|_{L^{2}(Q_{\tau})}+\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}}(\tau,\mathcal{M}(\tau)\setminus Q_{\tau})

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))e^{-\gamma_{n,k}\tau}\|\psi_{0}-\psi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}\,.

Here we use the facts that
\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}}(\tau,\mathcal{M}(\tau)\setminus Q_{\tau})=O(e^{-\tau^{2}/16})
since
\lambda[\mathcal{M}]<+\infty
, and
\|\psi_{0}-\psi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}\neq 0
since
\psi^{\prime}\neq\psi_{\mathbb{S}}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}}(\infty,\mathcal{M})=\lim_{\tau\to+\infty}\ln\left(\frac{\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})}{\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}^{\prime}}(\tau+1,\mathcal{M})}\right)=\gamma_{n,k}\,.

This proves the first equality in the Claim.

To prove the last inequality in the Claim, we may assume WLOG that
\mathcal{M}
doesn’t coincide with the low spherical flow given by
\mathbf{u}
. We first argue that
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\infty,\mathcal{M})>0
. Suppose for contradiction that this is not true, then let
\breve{\gamma}:=\gamma_{n,k}/2
, we know that for some
\tau_{\circ}\gg 1
, there holds
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\mathcal{M})\leq\breve{\gamma}
for every
\tau\geq\tau_{\circ}
.
Then for every integer
\ell\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{d}_{n,k}(\tau_{\circ}+\ell,\mathcal{M})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau_{\circ}+\ell,\mathcal{M})-C(\mathcal{M},\chi)\|\varphi_{\mathbf{u}}(\cdot,\tau_{\circ}+\ell)\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\geq\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau_{\circ},\mathcal{M})e^{-\breve{\gamma}\ell}-C(\mathcal{M},\chi)e^{-\gamma_{n,k}\ell}

\displaystyle\gtrsim_{\mathcal{M},\tau_{\circ}}e^{-\breve{\gamma}\ell}\,.

where the first inequality follows from (
3.14
) of Lemma (
3.7
), the second inequality follows from
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}\leq\breve{\gamma}
and Lemma
3.4
, and the last inequality holds when
\ell\gg 1
since
\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau_{\circ},\mathcal{M})\neq 0
. Taking
\ell\to\infty
, this contradicts to that
\mathcal{N}_{n,k}(\infty,\mathcal{M})\geq\gamma_{n,k}
.

Finally, we shall argue that if
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\infty,\mathcal{M})=\gamma_{n,k}
, then
\gamma_{n,k}=1/2
. Note that since
1/2
is the second largest positive eigenvalue in
\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})
, this will finish the proof of the last inequality in the Claim.
Since
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\infty,\mathcal{M})=\gamma_{n,k}
, by taking
\delta_{\ref{Thm_Asymp Profile(New)_gamma>0}}(n,\varepsilon)\ll 1
and applying Lemma
4.3
and
4.4
to
\mathcal{M},\mathbf{u}
,
\mu=\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\infty,\mathcal{M})(=\gamma_{n,k})
, we see that

\|(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})(\cdot,\tau)-e^{-\gamma_{n,k}\tau}\psi_{1}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-(2\gamma_{n,k}-3\varepsilon)\tau}

for some nonzero eigenfunction
\psi_{1}\in\mathrm{W}_{\gamma_{n,k}}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})
. Combining this with (
4.21
) and (
4.22
) gives

e^{-\gamma_{n,k}\tau}\|\psi_{0}-\psi_{\mathbb{S}}-\psi_{1}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-(2\gamma_{n,k}-3\varepsilon)\tau}+e^{-\frac{3}{2}\gamma_{n,k}\tau}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-(\gamma_{n,k}+\varepsilon)\tau}\,.

Sending
\tau\to+\infty
we derive
\psi_{0}=\psi_{\mathbb{S}}+\psi_{1}
. Since
\psi_{\mathbb{S}}
is the orthogonal projection of
\psi_{0}
onto
\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{S}}
and
\psi_{1}\neq 0
, we conclude that
\mathrm{W}_{\gamma_{n,k}}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})\setminus\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{S}}\neq\emptyset
. By the discussion in Section
2.3.3
, this forces
n-k=2
and therefore
\gamma_{n,k}=1/2
.
∎

∎

4.3.
Proof of Theorem
1.4

We finish this section by proving our main theorem of asymptotic profile. We may assume without loss of generality that
\mathcal{M}(\tau)\neq\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
, otherwise case
(iii)
holds automatically with
\varphi_{\mathbf{u}}\equiv 0
. Let
\tau\mapsto\mathcal{M}(\tau)
be the RMCF specified in Theorem
1.4
with
1
-graphical radius
\mathbf{r}_{1}(\mathcal{M}(\tau))
and graphical function
v(\cdot,\tau)
over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
. By Corollary
3.15
,
\mathcal{N}_{n,k}(\infty,\mathcal{M})\in(\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})\cap\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0})\cup\{+\infty\}
.

When
\mathcal{N}_{n,k}(\infty,\mathcal{M})=0
, to prove the desired asymptotic formula as in item
(i)
of Theorem
1.4
, in terms of
[
SX25b
, Theorem 1.2]
, we only need to show that
\mathcal{I}\neq\emptyset
. We prove this by contradiction: suppose
\mathcal{I}=\emptyset
. Then
[
SX25b
, Proposition 7.2]
shows that there exist a dimensional constant
K_{0}(n)>0
and
\epsilon>0
(possibly depends on
\mathcal{M}
a priori) such that for
\tau\gg 1
,
\mathcal{M}(\tau)
is a graph of function
v(\cdot,\tau)
over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
in
Q_{K_{0}\sqrt{\tau}}
,
v\neq 0
and

\lim_{\tau\to+\infty}e^{\epsilon\tau}\|v(\cdot,\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{K_{0}\sqrt{\tau}})}=0\,.

Hence by Proposition
2.4
and item
(iii)
of Lemma
C.1
,

\lim_{\tau\to+\infty}e^{\epsilon\tau}\mathbf{d}_{n,k}(\tau,\mathcal{M})\lesssim_{n}\limsup_{\tau\to+\infty}e^{\epsilon\tau}\left(\|v(\cdot,\tau)\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{K_{0}\sqrt{\tau}})}+\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{M}(\tau)\setminus Q_{K_{0}\sqrt{\tau}}]\right)=0\,.

On the other hand,
\mathcal{N}_{n,k}(\infty,\mathcal{M})=0
implies that when
\tau\geq\tau_{\circ}\gg 1
,
\mathcal{N}_{n,k}(\tau,\mathcal{M})<\epsilon
, and hence by definition of decay order, for every integer
l\geq 0
,

\mathbf{d}_{n,k}(\tau_{\circ}+l,\mathcal{M})\geq e^{-\epsilon l}\mathbf{d}_{n,k}(\tau_{\circ},\mathcal{M})\,.

which is a contradiction.

When
\mathcal{N}_{n,k}(\infty,\mathcal{M})>0
and
\mathcal{N}_{\mathscr{U}}(\infty,\mathcal{M})<+\infty
, we take
\gamma:=\mathcal{N}_{\mathscr{U}}(\infty,\mathcal{M})
. For every
\varepsilon\in(0,\gamma)
, let
\varepsilon^{\prime}:=\min\{1/4n,\varepsilon/3,1/(\gamma+1),\lambda[\mathcal{M}]^{-1}\}
and take
T_{\circ}\gg 1
such that
\mathcal{M}
is
\delta_{\ref{Thm_Asymp Profile(New)_gamma>0}}(n,\varepsilon^{\prime})
-
L^{2}
close to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
over
[T_{\circ},+\infty)
. We can then apply Theorem
4.1
to
\tau\mapsto\mathcal{M}(T_{\circ}+\tau),\gamma,\varepsilon^{\prime}
and conclude that there’s a unique low spherical flow
\breve{\mathbf{u}}\in\mathscr{U}
and eigenfunction
\breve{\psi}\in\mathrm{W}_{\gamma}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})
such that

\mathbf{r}_{1}(\mathcal{M}(T_{\circ}+\tau))\geq 2n+e^{\frac{\gamma-\varepsilon/3}{2(\gamma+1)}\cdot\tau}\geq e^{\frac{\gamma-\varepsilon}{2(\gamma+1)}\cdot(\tau+T_{\circ})}\,;

where the second inequality holds for
\tau\gg 1
; and that for
\tau\geq 1
,

\|v(\cdot,T_{\circ}+\tau)-\varphi_{\breve{\mathbf{u}}}(\cdot,\tau)-e^{-\gamma\tau}\breve{\psi}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon^{\prime}}e^{-(\gamma^{+}-3\varepsilon^{\prime})\tau}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon^{\prime},T_{\circ}}e^{-(\gamma^{+}-\varepsilon)(T_{\circ}+\tau)}\,.

This proves case
(ii)
of Theorem
1.4
with
\mathbf{u}
being such that
\varphi_{\mathbf{u}}(\cdot,\tau):=\varphi_{\breve{\mathbf{u}}}(\cdot,\tau-T_{\circ})
and
\psi:=e^{\gamma T_{\circ}}\breve{\psi}
.

When
\mathcal{N}_{n,k}(\infty,\mathcal{M})>0
and
\mathcal{N}_{\mathscr{U}}(\infty,\mathcal{M})=+\infty
. By (
4.2
) of Theorem
4.1
, there exists a unique low spherical flow
\mathbf{u}
such that
\mathcal{N}_{\mathscr{U}}(\infty,\mathcal{M})=\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{u}}(\infty,\mathcal{M})
. And for every
\lambda>1
, take
\varepsilon_{\lambda}:=\min\{1/4n,\lambda[\mathcal{M}]^{-1},1/(2\lambda+1)\}
, and let
T_{\lambda}\gg 1
be such that
\mathcal{M}
is
\delta_{\ref{Thm_Asymp Profile(New)_gamma>0}}(n,\varepsilon_{\lambda})
-
L^{2}
close to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
over
[T_{\lambda},+\infty)
. We can then apply Theorem
4.1
with
\tau\mapsto\mathcal{M}(T_{\lambda}+\tau),2\lambda,\varepsilon_{\lambda}
in place of
\mathcal{M},\gamma,\varepsilon
therein to conclude that

\mathbf{r}_{1}(\mathcal{M}(T_{\lambda}+\tau))\geq 2n+e^{\frac{2\lambda-\varepsilon_{\lambda}}{2(2\lambda+1)}\cdot\tau}\geq e^{\frac{\lambda}{2(\lambda+1)}\cdot(T_{\lambda}+\tau)}\,;

where the second inequality holds for
\tau\gg 1
; and that for
\tau\geq 1
,

\|(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})(\cdot,T_{\lambda}+\tau)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon_{\lambda}}e^{-(\lambda^{+}-3\varepsilon_{\lambda})\tau}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon_{\lambda},T_{\lambda}}e^{-\lambda(T_{\lambda}+\tau)}\,.

This proves case
(iii)
of Theorem
1.4
.

5.
Estimate of Whitney data

Throughout this section, let
\varepsilon\in(0,1/(2n))
;
t\mapsto\mathbf{M}(t)
be a MCF in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
on
[-e,+\infty)
.
Suppose

•

\lambda[\mathbf{M}]\leq\varepsilon^{-1}
;

•

(\mathbf{0},0)\in\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})_{+}
, with tangent flow of
\mathbf{M}
to be
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
;

•

the RMCF
\tau\mapsto\mathcal{M}(\tau)
of
\mathbf{M}
based at
(\mathbf{0},0)
is
\delta
-
L^{2}
close to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
on
[-1,+\infty)
.

For every
p\in\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})_{+}
, we associate the following data:

(i)

\mathcal{C}_{p}\in\operatorname{Rot}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})
be the tangent flow of
\mathbf{M}
at
p
, still parametrized by
(\theta,y)
, where
y
is the coordinate on
\operatorname{spine}(\mathcal{C}_{p})
and
\theta
is the coordinate on the spherical part
\mathcal{C}_{p}\cap\operatorname{spine}(\mathcal{C}_{p})^{\perp}
;

(ii)

\mathbf{L}_{p}:\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\to\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
be the linear orthogonal projection onto
\operatorname{spine}(\mathcal{C}_{p})=:|\mathbf{L}_{p}|
;

(iii)

\mathbf{q}_{p}:|\mathbf{L}_{p}|\to|\mathbf{L}_{p}|^{\perp}
be the quadratic polynomial determined by the asymptotic profile
\psi_{p}\in\mathrm{W}_{1/2}(\mathcal{C}_{p})
as in Remark
4.2
, and denote for later reference
\mathbf{Q}_{p}:=\mathbf{q}_{p}\circ\mathbf{L}_{p}
, which is an
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
-valued quadratic polynomial on
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
.

We will use
\|\cdot\|
to denote the operator norm of linear or quadratic
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
-valued maps from
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
.

Recall that

(5.1)

\displaystyle\gamma_{n,k}^{+}:=\min\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})\cap(\frac{1}{2},+\infty)=\frac{1}{2}+\min\{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{n-k}\}\,.

Also let
\mathbf{L}_{\circ}=\{0\}\times\mathbb{R}^{k},\mathbf{u}_{\circ},\mathbf{q}_{\circ},\mathbf{Q}_{\circ}
be the data associated to the spacetime origin
(\mathbf{0},0)
.
The goal of this section is the following location estimate on the data
\bar{p},\ \bar{\mathbf{L}}:=\mathbf{L}_{\bar{p}},\ \bar{\mathbf{u}}:=\mathbf{u}_{\bar{p}},\ \bar{\mathbf{q}}:=\mathbf{q}_{\bar{p}},\ \bar{\mathbf{Q}}:=\mathbf{Q}_{\bar{p}}
when
\bar{p}\in\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})_{+}
is close to
(\mathbf{0},0)
.

Lemma 5.1
.

For every
\varepsilon\in(0,1/(10n))
, there exists
\delta_{\ref{Lem_C^2 Reg_Main}}(n,\varepsilon)\in(0,\varepsilon)
such that if
\mathbf{M}
is as above, let
\bar{p}=(\bar{x},\bar{y},\bar{t})\in\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})_{+}
(where
\bar{x}\in|\mathbf{L}_{\circ}|^{\perp},\bar{y}\in|\mathbf{L}_{\circ}|
) be such that

r:=\max\{|\bar{x}|,|\bar{y}|,|\bar{t}|^{1/2}\}\ \ \in(0,\delta_{\ref{Lem_C^2 Reg_Main}}]\,.

Then we have
\|\mathbf{Q}_{\circ}\|,\|\bar{\mathbf{Q}}\|\leq 1
,
|\bar{\mathbf{L}}|=e^{\bar{\mathbf{A}}}(|\mathbf{L}_{\circ}|)
for some
\bar{\mathbf{A}}\in\mathfrak{g}_{n,k}^{\perp}
of form
\bar{\mathbf{A}}=\begin{bmatrix}0&\bar{\mathbf{\ell}}\\
-\bar{\mathbf{\ell}}^{\top}&0\end{bmatrix}
and

(5.2)

\displaystyle r^{-2}|\bar{t}|+r^{-1}|\bar{x}-\mathbf{q}_{\circ}(\bar{y})|+\|\bar{\mathbf{\ell}}-\nabla\mathbf{q}_{\circ}(\bar{y})\|+r\|\bar{\mathbf{Q}}-\mathbf{Q}_{\circ}\|\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}r^{2\gamma_{n,k}^{+}-10\varepsilon}\,.

Note that in view of the Whitney Extension Theorem, applying this Lemma (possibly with a translation and rotation) to every pair of points in
\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})_{+}
that are sufficiently close to each other proves the
C^{2,\alpha}
-regularity of
\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})_{+}
. We shall proceed with the details in the next section.

Proof.

Step 1 (A priori bounds on Whitney data).

Suppose the tangent flow of
\mathbf{M}
at
\bar{p}
is
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Rot}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})
. First note that by a compactness argument and Colding-Minicozzi’s quantitative uniqueness of cylindrical tangent flow
[
CM25
]
,
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is close to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
as long as we take
\delta_{\ref{Lem_C^2 Reg_Main}}(n,\varepsilon)\ll 1
, and hence there exists a unique
\bar{\mathbf{A}}\in\mathfrak{g}_{n,k}^{\perp}
such that
e^{\bar{\mathbf{A}}}(|\mathbf{L}_{\circ}|)=|\bar{\mathbf{L}}|
, with estimates,

(5.3)

\displaystyle r^{-2}|\bar{t}|+r^{-1}|\bar{x}|+|\bar{\mathbf{A}}|\leq\Psi(\delta|n,\varepsilon)\,.

And hence, by definition of
r
(and taking
\delta_{\ref{Lem_C^2 Reg_Main}}(n,\varepsilon)
smaller), we have,

(5.4)

\displaystyle|\bar{y}|=r\,.

Step 2 (Estimates from Theorem
4.1
).

Let
v(\cdot,\tau),\,\bar{v}(\cdot,\tau)
be the graphical function of
\mathcal{M}(\tau)
and
\bar{\mathcal{M}}(\tau):=(e^{-\bar{\mathbf{A}}})_{\sharp}\mathcal{M}^{\bar{p}}(\tau)
over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
respectively. Note that by taking
\delta_{\ref{Lem_C^2 Reg_Main}}(n,\varepsilon)\ll 1
, assumptions
(I)
,
(II)
in Theorem
4.1
are satisfied by
\mathcal{M}
and
\bar{\mathcal{M}}
with
\delta\leq\delta_{\ref{Thm_Asymp Profile(New)_gamma>0}},\gamma=1/2,\varepsilon
, with
\gamma^{+}=\gamma^{+}_{n,k}
.
Hence, by Theorem
4.1
, for every
\tau\geq 1
and every
2n\leq R\leq 2n+e^{\tau/12}
, we have

(5.5)

\displaystyle\|v\|_{C^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{R})}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-(\min\{1/2,1/(n-k)\}-\varepsilon)\tau}R^{3}\,;

and the asymptotic expansions for
v,\bar{v}
:

(5.6)

\displaystyle\begin{split}v(\cdot,\tau)&=\varphi_{\mathbf{u}_{\circ}}(\cdot,\tau)+e^{-\tau/2}\psi_{\circ}+\mathcal{E}_{\circ}(\cdot,\tau)\,,\\
\bar{v}(\cdot,\tau)&=\varphi_{\bar{\mathbf{u}}}(\cdot,\tau)+e^{-\tau/2}\bar{\psi}+\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\cdot,\tau)\,,\\
\|\mathcal{E}_{\circ}(\cdot,\tau)\|_{C^{0}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{R})}&+\|\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\cdot,\tau)\|_{C^{0}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{R})}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-(\gamma^{+}_{n,k}-\varepsilon)\tau}R^{2(2+\gamma^{+}_{n,k})}\,;\end{split}

for some low spherical flows
\mathbf{u}_{\circ},\ \bar{\mathbf{u}}\in\mathscr{U}
and eigenfunctions
\psi_{\circ},\ \bar{\psi}\in\mathrm{W}_{1/2}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})
with estimate
\|\psi_{\circ}\|_{L^{2}},\|\bar{\psi}\|_{L^{2}}\leq 1
. (Note that
\bar{\psi}
is related with the Whitney data at
\bar{p}
by
\bar{\psi}=\psi_{\bar{p}}\circ e^{\bar{\mathbf{A}}}
).

Step 3 (Relation between
v
and
\bar{v}
).
Recall that

\mathcal{M}^{\bar{p}}(\tau)=\sqrt{1-\bar{t}e^{\tau}}\cdot\mathcal{M}\left(\tau-\log(1-\bar{t}e^{\tau})\right)-e^{\tau/2}(\bar{x},\bar{y})\,.

We shall apply Lemma
C.1

(iv)
to compare
v
and
\bar{v}
at
\tau_{r}:=-2\ln r
time slice. We set,

(5.7)

\displaystyle\bar{\lambda}_{r}=\sqrt{1-\bar{t}e^{\tau_{r}}},

\displaystyle(\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{r},\bar{\mathbf{y}}_{r}):=(e^{\tau_{r}/2}\bar{x},e^{\tau_{r}/2}\bar{y})\,.

Note that by (
5.3
), (
5.4
) and taking
\delta_{\ref{Lem_C^2 Reg_Main}}(n,\varepsilon)\ll 1
, we have

\displaystyle|\bar{\lambda}_{r}-1|\sim r^{-2}|\bar{t}|\ll 1\,,

\displaystyle|\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{r}|=r^{-1}|\bar{x}|\ll 1\,,

\displaystyle|\bar{\mathbf{y}}_{r}|=1\,.

Claim.

Let
\Lambda=\Lambda_{\ref{Cor_L^2 Mono_Low Bd Upsilon_(u,u'; w)}}(n,1/2)>2n
. By taking
\delta_{\ref{Lem_C^2 Reg_Main}}(n,\varepsilon)\ll 1
, we have
\mathbf{r}_{1}(\mathcal{M}(\tau_{r}))>4\Lambda
and,

(5.8)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bar{v}(\cdot,\tau_{r})=v(\cdot+(0,\bar{\lambda}_{r}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{y}}_{r}),\ \tau_{r})+\Upsilon_{r}+\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\,,\end{split}

in
Q_{\Lambda}
, where
\Upsilon_{r}
is the sum of three eigenfunctions of
-L_{n,k}
:

(5.9)

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{r}:=\varrho(\bar{\lambda}_{r}-1)-\psi_{\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{r}}-\psi_{\bar{\mathbf{A}}}\,;

and the error estimate holds,

(5.10)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{\Lambda})}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}\left(r^{2\gamma^{+}_{n,k}-1-4\varepsilon}+|\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{r}|+|\bar{\lambda}_{r}-1|+|\bar{\mathbf{A}}|\right)\left(|\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{r}|+|\bar{\lambda}_{r}-1|+|\bar{\mathbf{A}}|\right)\,.

Proof of Claim.

By definition of
\bar{\mathcal{M}}
in
Step 2
,

\bar{\mathcal{M}}(\tau_{r})=(e^{-\bar{\mathbf{A}}})_{\sharp}\left(\bar{\lambda}_{r}\cdot\mathcal{M}(\tau_{r}-2\log\bar{\lambda}_{r})-(\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{r},\bar{\mathbf{y}}_{r})\right),

while by (
5.3
), (
5.4
) and (
5.5
), by taking
\delta_{\ref{Lem_C^2 Reg_Main}}(n,\varepsilon)\ll 1
we have,

(5.11)

\displaystyle\|v\|_{C^{2}((\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{2\Lambda}(0,\bar{\lambda}_{r}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{y}}_{r}))\times[\tau_{r}-1,\tau_{r}])}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}r^{2\gamma^{+}_{n,k}-1-2\varepsilon}<\kappa^{\prime}_{\ref{Lem_App_Graph over Cylinder}}(n)/2\,;

\displaystyle|\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{r}|+(|\bar{\lambda}_{r}-1|+|\bar{\mathbf{A}}|)\Lambda\leq\Psi(\delta|n,\varepsilon)<\kappa^{\prime}_{\ref{Lem_App_Graph over Cylinder}}(n)/2\,.

Hence item
(iv)
of Lemma
C.1
applies to give

\displaystyle\bar{v}(\cdot,\tau_{r})=v(\cdot+(0,\bar{\lambda}_{r}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{y}}_{r}),\ \tau_{r}-2\log\bar{\lambda}_{r})+\Upsilon_{r}+\breve{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}=v(\cdot+(0,\bar{\lambda}_{r}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{y}}_{r}),\ \tau_{r})+\Upsilon_{r}+\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\,,

which implies (
5.8
) with error estimate

(5.12)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\|_{C^{0}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{\Lambda})}&\leq\|\breve{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}\|_{C^{0}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{\Lambda})}+2|\log\bar{\lambda}_{r}|\cdot\|\partial_{\tau}v\|_{C^{0}((\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{\Lambda}(0,\bar{\lambda}_{r}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{y}}_{r}))\times[\tau^{\prime}_{r}-2\log\bar{\lambda}_{r},\tau^{\prime}_{r}])}\\
&\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}\left(\|v\|_{C^{2}((\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{\Lambda+1}(0,\bar{\lambda}_{r}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{y}}_{r}))\times[\tau^{\prime}_{r}-1,\tau^{\prime}_{r}])}+\|\Upsilon_{r}\|_{L^{2}}\right)\|\Upsilon_{r}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}\left(r^{2\gamma^{+}_{n,k}-1-4\varepsilon}+|\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{r}|+|\bar{\lambda}_{r}-1|+|\bar{\mathbf{A}}|\right)\left(|\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{r}|+|\bar{\lambda}_{r}-1|+|\bar{\mathbf{A}}|\right)\,.\end{split}

Here the second inequality follows from Lemma
C.1

(iv)
and the fact that

\|\Upsilon_{r}\|_{L^{2}}\sim_{n}|\bar{\lambda}_{r}-1|+|\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{r}|+|\bar{\mathbf{A}}|\,,

and the third inequality follows from (
5.11
).
∎

Step 4.
Combining (
5.6
) at
\tau:=\tau_{r}
time slice, (
5.8
) and (
5.10
), we shall derive the following estimate, which leads to the final estimate on the Whitney data in the next step.

(5.13)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|(\varphi_{\mathbf{u}_{\circ}}-\varphi_{\bar{\mathbf{u}}})(\cdot,\tau_{r})\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|e^{-\tau_{r}/2}\left(\psi_{\circ}(\cdot+(0,\bar{\lambda}_{r}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{y}}_{r}))-\bar{\psi}\right)+\Upsilon_{r}\right\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\qquad\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}r^{2\gamma^{+}_{n,k}-2\varepsilon}+\left(r^{2\gamma^{+}_{n,k}-1-4\varepsilon}+|\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{r}|+|\bar{\lambda}_{r}-1|+|\bar{\mathbf{A}}|\right)\left(|\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{r}|+|\bar{\lambda}_{r}-1|+|\bar{\mathbf{A}}|\right)\,.\end{split}

Proof of (
5.13
).

By (
5.6
) at
\tau:=\tau_{r}
time slice and (
5.8
), we have in
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{\Lambda}
,

\displaystyle\left(\varphi_{\bar{\mathbf{u}}}(\cdot,\tau_{r})+e^{-\tau_{r}/2}\bar{\psi}+\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\cdot,\tau_{r})\right)-\left(\Upsilon_{r}+\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\varphi_{\mathbf{u}_{\circ}}(\cdot,\tau_{r})+e^{-\tau_{r}/2}\psi_{\circ}(\cdot+(0,\bar{\lambda}_{r}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{y}}_{r}))+\mathcal{E}_{\circ}(\cdot+(0,\bar{\lambda}_{r}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{y}}_{r}),\tau_{r})\,.

Moving
\mathcal{E}_{\circ},\bar{\mathcal{E}},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
to the same side, integrating over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{\Lambda}
(with respect to Gaussian weight) and applying (
5.6
) and (
5.10
) yield,

(5.14)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|(\varphi_{\mathbf{u}_{\circ}}-\varphi_{\bar{\mathbf{u}}})(\cdot,\tau_{r})+e^{-\tau_{r}/2}\left(\psi_{\circ}(\cdot+(0,\bar{\lambda}_{r}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{y}}_{r}))-\bar{\psi}\right)+\Upsilon_{r}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{\Lambda})}\\
&\qquad\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}\left\|\mathcal{E}_{\circ}(\cdot+(0,\bar{\lambda}_{r}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{y}}_{r}),\tau_{r})\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{\Lambda})}+\|\bar{\mathcal{E}}(\cdot,\tau_{r})\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{\Lambda})}+\|\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{\Lambda})}\\
&\qquad\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}r^{2\gamma^{+}_{n,k}-2\varepsilon}+\left(r^{2\gamma^{+}_{n,k}-1-4\varepsilon}+|\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{r}|+|\bar{\lambda}_{r}-1|+|\bar{\mathbf{A}}|\right)\left(|\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{r}|+|\bar{\lambda}_{r}-1|+|\bar{\mathbf{A}}|\right)\,.\end{split}

On the other hand, since the second and third terms on the left hand side above are linear combinations of eigenfunctions of
-L_{n,k}
with eigenvalue
\leq 1/2
and perpendicular to the low spherical mode
\mathrm{W}_{\mathbb{S}}
, by Corollary
3.6
and the choice of
\Lambda
in
Step 3
, we have

\displaystyle\text{LHS of \eqref{Equ_Improve_Pf in Step 4}}\gtrsim_{n}\left\|(\varphi_{\mathbf{u}_{\circ}}-\varphi_{\bar{\mathbf{u}}})(\cdot,\tau_{r})\right\|_{L^{2}}+\left\|e^{-\tau_{r}/2}\left(\psi_{\circ}(\cdot+(0,\bar{\lambda}_{r}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{y}}_{r}))-\bar{\psi}\right)+\Upsilon_{r}\right\|_{L^{2}}\,.

Together with (
5.14
), this finishes the proof.
∎

Step 5 ((
5.13
) yields the location estimates).

We first prove a geometric estimate,

(5.15)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|e^{-\tau_{r}/2}\left(\psi_{\circ}(\cdot+(0,\bar{\lambda}_{r}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{y}}_{r}))-\bar{\psi}\right)+\Upsilon_{r}\right\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\qquad\gtrsim_{n}\ e^{\tau_{r}/2}|\mathbf{q}_{\circ}(\bar{\lambda}_{r}^{-1}\bar{y})-\bar{x}|+\|\nabla\mathbf{q}_{\circ}(\bar{\lambda}_{r}^{-1}\bar{y})-\bar{\mathbf{\ell}}\|+|\bar{\lambda}_{r}-1|+e^{-\tau_{r}/2}\|\psi_{\circ}-\bar{\psi}\|_{L^{2}}\,.\end{split}

Proof of (
5.15
).

By the definition
(iii)
of
\mathbf{q}_{p}
, we can set

\psi_{\circ}(\theta,y)=\left\langle\mathbf{q}_{\circ}(y)-2\tr\mathbf{q}_{\circ}(y),\hat{\theta}\right\rangle+\mathbf{c}_{\circ}(y),

where
\mathbf{c}_{\circ}
is a (possibly
0
) cubic Hermitian polynomial on
\mathbb{R}^{k}
. Then for every
0\neq\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
,

\displaystyle\psi(\theta,y+\mathbf{y})-\psi(\theta,y)

\displaystyle=\left\langle\mathbf{q}_{\circ}(y+\mathbf{y})-\mathbf{q}_{\circ}(y),\hat{\theta}\right\rangle+(\mathbf{c}_{\circ}(y+\mathbf{y})-\mathbf{c}_{\circ}(y))

\displaystyle=\left\langle\mathbf{q}_{\circ}(\mathbf{y})+\nabla\mathbf{q}_{\circ}(\mathbf{y})\cdot y,\hat{\theta}\right\rangle+(\mathbf{c}_{\circ}(y+\mathbf{y})-\mathbf{c}_{\circ}(y))\,.

Hence combined with (
5.7
) and (
5.9
), we have (denote for simplicity
\tilde{y}_{r}:=\bar{\lambda}_{r}^{-1}\bar{y}=e^{-\tau_{r}/2}\bar{\lambda}_{r}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{y}}_{r}
)

\displaystyle\left(e^{-\tau_{r}/2}\left(\psi_{\circ}(\cdot+(0,\bar{\lambda}_{r}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{y}}_{r}))-\bar{\psi}\right)+\Upsilon_{r}\right)(\theta,y)

\displaystyle\qquad=e^{-\tau_{r}/2}(\psi_{\circ}-\bar{\psi})(\theta,y)+e^{\tau_{r}/2}\left\langle\mathbf{q}_{\circ}(\tilde{y}_{r})-\bar{x},\hat{\theta}\right\rangle+\left\langle(\nabla\mathbf{q}_{\circ}(\tilde{y}_{r})-\bar{\mathbf{\ell}})y,\hat{\theta}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\ +\left(\varrho(\bar{\lambda}_{r}-1)+\mathbf{c}_{\circ}(y+\bar{\lambda}_{r}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{y}}_{r})-\mathbf{c}_{\circ}(y)\right)\,,

where recall that we set
\bar{\mathbf{A}}=\begin{bmatrix}0&\bar{\mathbf{\ell}}\\
-\bar{\mathbf{\ell}}^{\top}&0\end{bmatrix}
.
Note that as a linear combination of eigenfunctions of
-L_{n,k}
on
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
, the four terms on the right-hand side above are pairwise orthogonal to each other. Together with Lemma
2.5
, this implies (
5.15
).
∎

Now combining (
5.15
) with (
5.13
) and (
5.7
), we derive (denote as above
\tilde{y}_{r}:=\bar{\lambda}_{r}^{-1}\bar{y}
and recall
\tau_{r}=-2\ln r
)

(5.16)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&|\bar{\lambda}_{r}-1|+r^{-1}|\mathbf{q}_{\circ}(\tilde{y}_{r})-\bar{x}|+\|\nabla\mathbf{q}_{\circ}(\tilde{y}_{r})-\bar{\mathbf{\ell}}\|+r\|\psi_{\circ}-\bar{\psi}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\qquad\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}r^{2\gamma^{+}_{n,k}-2\varepsilon}+\left(r^{2\gamma^{+}_{n,k}-1-4\varepsilon}+r^{-1}|\bar{x}|+|\bar{\lambda}_{r}-1|+|\bar{\mathbf{A}}|\right)\left(r^{-1}|\bar{x}|+|\bar{\lambda}_{r}-1|+|\bar{\mathbf{A}}|\right).\end{split}

Recall in
Step 2
we get
\|\psi_{\circ}\|_{L^{2}},\|\bar{\psi}\|_{L^{2}}\leq 1
, and
\mathbf{q}_{\circ}
is a quadratic form, the remaining terms on the right-hand side have estimates,

\displaystyle r^{-1}|\bar{x}|

\displaystyle\leq r^{-1}|\mathbf{q}_{\circ}(\tilde{y}_{r})-\bar{x}|+r^{-1}\|\mathbf{q}_{\circ}\|\cdot|\tilde{y}_{r}|^{2}\leq r^{-1}|\mathbf{q}_{\circ}(\tilde{y}_{r})-\bar{x}|+2r\,,

\displaystyle|\bar{\mathbf{A}}|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n}\|\bar{\mathbf{\ell}}\|\leq\|\nabla\mathbf{q}_{\circ}(\tilde{y}_{r})-\bar{\mathbf{\ell}}\|+\|\nabla\mathbf{q}_{\circ}\|\cdot|\tilde{y}_{r}|\lesssim_{n}\|\nabla\mathbf{q}_{\circ}(\tilde{y}_{r})-\bar{\mathbf{\ell}}\|+r\,.

Using these (combining with (
5.3
)), by taking
\delta_{\ref{Lem_C^2 Reg_Main}}(n,\varepsilon)\ll 1
, one can absorb the second term on the right-hand side of (
5.16
) by its left hand side and get,

(5.17)

\displaystyle|\bar{\lambda}_{r}-1|+r^{-1}|\mathbf{q}_{\circ}(\tilde{y}_{r})-\bar{x}|+\|\nabla\mathbf{q}_{\circ}(\tilde{y}_{r})-\bar{\mathbf{\ell}}\|+r\|\psi_{\circ}-\bar{\psi}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}r^{2\gamma^{+}_{n,k}-4\varepsilon}\,.

Recall that
|\bar{\lambda}_{r}-1|\sim r^{-2}|\bar{t}|
and
|\tilde{y}_{r}-\bar{y}|\lesssim r|\bar{\lambda}_{r}-1|
and
\bar{\psi}=\psi_{\bar{p}}\circ e^{\bar{\mathbf{A}}}
. This proves the desired estimate (
5.2
).
∎

6.
Proof of Theorem
1.1

In this section, we collected all the ingredients from the previous sections to prove Theorem
1.1
. Throughout this section, we let
1\leq k\leq n-1
,
\mathbf{M}
is a mean curvature flow in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
. We will further assume
\mathbf{M}
has entropy upper bound by
\varepsilon^{-1}
for some small
\varepsilon\in(0,1/10n)
.

For part
(i)
of Theorem
1.1
, the Hausdorff dimension estimate of
\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})_{0}
will be proved in Section
6.1
simply by a blow-up argument. When
k=1
, the singularities in
\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})_{0}
must be nondegenerate, and hence the isolatedness has been proved in
[
SWX25
]
.

Part
(ii)
of Theorem
1.1
is a direct consequence of the upper-semi-continuity of decay order at
\tau=\infty
, to be discussed in Section
6.2
.

Part
(iii)
and
(iv)
of Theorem
1.1
will be proved in Section
6.3
based on the location estimate in Lemma
5.1
. In fact, we establish a general result to get
C^{2,\alpha}
regularity from the location estimate like (
5.2
) using the Whitney extension theorem, see Proposition
6.6
.

6.1.
Hausdorff dimension of partially nondegenerate singularities

Recall the
C^{1}
-normal form theorem of cylindrical singularity.

Theorem 6.1

(
[
SX25b
, Theorem 1.4]
)
.

Suppose
\mathcal{M}
is the rescaled mean curvature flow of
\mathbf{M}
at a
k
-cylindrical singularity. Then for any
\mathbf{L}>0
, there exist a rotation
\mathbf{B}\in\mathrm{O}(n+1)
, an index set
\mathcal{I}\subset\{1,2,\cdots,k\}
and
\tau_{0}>0
such that for every
\tau>\tau_{0}
,
\mathbf{B}_{\sharp}\mathcal{M}(\tau)
can be written as a graph of function
v(\cdot,\tau)
over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
in
Q_{\mathbf{L}}
, and that

(6.1)

\left\|v(\theta,y,\tau)-\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\frac{\varrho}{4\tau}(y_{i}^{2}-2)\right\|_{C^{1}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{\mathbf{L}})}=o(\tau^{-1})\,.

In particular, if
\mathcal{I}=\{1,2,\cdots,k\}
, the singularity is called nondegenerate. In the following, we consider
|\mathcal{I}|=m
,
