1\leq m\leq k-1
.
We use
\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})_{0}^{m}
to denote the set of singularities of this type, and we call them
partially nondegenerate
.
For every
p\in\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})_{0}^{m}
, let
\mathcal{C}_{p}\in\operatorname{Rot}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})
be the tangent flow of
\mathbf{M}
at
p
. We use
V^{\mathrm{dg}}_{p}
to denote the
(k-m)
-dimensional subspace of
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
such that
\mathbf{B}(V_{p}^{\mathrm{dg}})=\text{span}\{\partial_{y_{i}}:i\in\{1,2,\cdots,k\}\backslash\mathcal{I}\}
. In other words,
V_{p}^{\mathrm{dg}}
denotes the subspace of the degenerate directions; also, we use
V^{\mathrm{ndg}}_{p}:=\operatorname{spine}(\mathcal{C}_{p})\cap(V_{p}^{\mathrm{dg}})^{\perp}
to denote the subspace of the nondegenerate directions, and use
\mathbf{y}_{p}^{\mathrm{ndg}}
to denote the projection of the vector
\mathbf{y}\in\operatorname{spine}(\mathcal{C}_{p})
to
V_{p}^{\mathrm{ndg}}
.
Since by definition,

\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})_{0}=\bigcup_{m=1}^{k-1}\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})_{0}^{m}\,,

part
(i)
of Theorem
1.1
is a direct consequence of the following dimension estimate of
\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})_{0}^{m}
.

Theorem 6.2
.

The parabolic Hausdorff dimension of
\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})_{0}^{m}
is at most
(k-m)
.

The key to proving this theorem is the following lemma asserting that
k
-cylindrical singularities converging to some
p\in\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})_{0}^{m}
are approaching in the degenerate direction
V_{p}^{\mathrm{dg}}
.

Lemma 6.3
.

Suppose
p=(\mathbf{0},0)\in\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})_{0}^{m}
is a partially nondegenerate singularity of
\mathbf{M}
with tangent flow
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
.
If
\{p_{j}=(x_{j},y_{j},t_{j})\}_{j\geq 1}\subset\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})
is a sequence of singularities converging to
(\mathbf{0},0)
as
j\to\infty
, such that
y_{j}\neq 0
and
y_{j}/|y_{j}|\to\hat{\mathbf{y}}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
. Then
\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{p}^{\mathrm{ndg}}=0
.

Proof.

Denote for simplicity
\tau\mapsto\mathcal{M}_{j}(\tau):=\mathcal{M}^{p_{j}}(\tau)
to be the RMCF of
\mathbf{M}
based at
p_{j}
. The following facts have essentially been proved in
[
SWX25
, Section 4]
, especially Claims 4.4 and 4.5. We refer the readers to the proof there. We fix
\mathbf{L}\gg 2n
to be determined. For
j\gg 1
, let

a_{j}:=2\log(|y_{j}|^{-1}\mathbf{L})\,.

Claim
.

There exists
\varepsilon_{2}(n,\varepsilon)\in(0,\varepsilon)
such that

(6.2)

\displaystyle\limsup_{j\to\infty}

\displaystyle\sup_{|\tau-a_{j}|\leq 3}\mathbf{d}_{n,k}(\mathcal{M}_{j}(\tau))=0\,;

(6.3)

\displaystyle\limsup_{j\to\infty}

\displaystyle\sup_{|\tau-a_{j}|\leq 3}\mathcal{N}_{n,k}(\tau,\mathcal{M}_{j})<\varepsilon_{2}^{-1}\,.

Claim
.

Let
v_{j}(\cdot,\tau)
be the graphical function of
\mathcal{M}_{j}(\tau)
in
Q_{\mathbf{L}}
and
0
-extended to an
L^{\infty}
function on
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
. Then

(6.4)

\displaystyle\limsup_{j\to\infty}\inf_{c>0,\ c^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}}\|c^{-1}v_{j}(\cdot,a_{j})-c^{\prime}-y\cdot\mathbf{y}_{p}^{\mathrm{ndg}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\Psi(\mathbf{L}^{-1}|n)\,.

Moreover, if
\mathbf{y}_{p}^{\mathrm{ndg}}\neq 0
, then

(6.5)

\displaystyle\limsup_{j\to\infty}\|v_{j}(\cdot,a_{j})\|_{L^{2}}^{-1}\cdot\|\Pi_{\leq-1/2}(v_{j}(\cdot,a_{j}))\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\Psi(\mathbf{L}^{-1}|n)\,.

Here recall
\Psi(\mathbf{L}^{-1}|n)
denotes a function that converges to
0
as
\mathbf{L}^{-1}\to 0
, and the speed converging to
0
is dimensional.

With these Claims, let
\bar{\delta}\in(0,\varepsilon_{2})
that is smaller than all the
\delta
’s throughout Section
3
,
\mathbf{L}(n,\varepsilon)\gg 1
be such that
\Psi(\mathbf{L}^{-1}|n)<\bar{\delta}
. Conclude from (
6.3
), (
6.5
), and Corollary
3.12
, if
\hat{\mathbf{y}}_{p}^{\mathrm{ndg}}\neq 0
, we have

\limsup_{j\to\infty}\sup_{\tau\in[a_{j},a_{j}+3]}\mathcal{N}(\tau,\mathcal{M}_{j})\leq-1/2+\varepsilon_{2}<-1/4\,.

The lemma then follows from Lemma
3.16
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
6.2
.

We prove by contradiction. Suppose
\mathcal{H}^{\beta}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})_{0}^{m})>0
for some
\beta>(k-m)
. Then by Lemma
2.1
with
E:=\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})_{0}^{m}
, there exists
p_{\circ}\in E
such that the accumulation set

\mathcal{A}_{E,p_{\circ}}:=\left\{z\in\overline{P_{1/2}}\,:\exists\,(j_{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1}\nearrow+\infty,(z_{\ell})_{\ell\geq 1}\text{ s.t. $z_{\ell}\in r_{j_{\ell}}^{-1}.(E-p_{\circ})\cap P_{1/2}$ and $z_{\ell}\to z$}\right\},

has
\mathcal{H}^{\beta}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{A}_{E,p_{\circ}})>0
. In the following, we shall prove that
\mathcal{A}_{E,p_{\circ}}\subset V^{\mathrm{dg}}_{p_{\circ}}\times\{0\}
, which is a contradiction to
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\beta}(\mathcal{A}_{E,p_{\circ}})>0
since
\dim V^{\mathrm{dg}}_{p_{\circ}}=k-m
. By a translation and rotation, we assume without loss of generality that
p_{\circ}=(\mathbf{0},0)
and the tangent flow of
\mathbf{M}
at
p_{\circ}
is
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
.

From its definition above, for any
(Z,s)=(x_{Z},y_{Z},s)\in\mathcal{A}_{E,p_{\circ}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n-k+1}\times\mathbb{R}^{k}\times\mathbb{R}
, there exists a sequence of singular points
p_{j}=(x_{j},y_{j},t_{j})
in
\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})_{0}^{m}\cap P_{1}((\mathbf{0},0))
such that as
j\to\infty
,

•

\max\{|(x_{j},y_{j})|,|t_{j}|^{1/2}\}=:r_{j}\searrow 0
;

•

(r_{j}^{-1}x_{j},r_{j}^{-1}y_{j},r_{j}^{-2}t_{j})\to(x_{Z},y_{Z},s)
.

We use
r_{j}^{-1}\mathbf{M}
to denote the mean curvature flow parabolic dilated by the factor
r_{j}^{-1}
. Then since
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
is the tangent flow of
\mathbf{M}
at
(\mathbf{0},0)
, the sequence of mean curvature flow
\{r_{j}^{-1}\mathbf{M}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
converges, in the sense of Brakke flow, to the shrinking cylinder
(t\mapsto\sqrt{-t}\,\mathcal{C}_{n,k})
. By the lower semi-continuity of the Gaussian density of singularities, we must have that
(x_{Z},y_{Z},s)=\lim\limits_{j\to\infty}(r_{j}^{-1}x_{j},r_{j}^{-1}y_{j},r_{j}^{-2}t_{j})
belongs to the singular set of
(t\mapsto\sqrt{-t}\,\mathcal{C}_{n,k})
, hence
x_{Z}=\mathbf{0}
and
s=0
. Finally, Lemma
6.3
shows that
(y_{Z})_{p_{\circ}}^{\mathrm{ndg}}=0
and hence
y_{Z}\in V_{p_{\circ}}^{\mathrm{dg}}
. This finises the proof that
\mathcal{A}_{E,p_{\circ}}\subset V^{\mathrm{dg}}_{p_{\circ}}\times\{0\}
.
∎

Remark 6.4
.

While the major goal of this section is to study partially nondegenerate singularities, the idea that analyzing the singular sets by slightly translating the flow in the spacetime can be used to study singularities with various asymptotics. For example, using the same method as this section, one can show that if the leading asymptotics of the RMCF at the origin is
h_{3}(y_{j})=c(y_{j}^{3}-6y_{j})
, for some
j\in\{1,2,\cdots,k\}
, the singular set can not approach the origin from the
y_{j}
-direction.

More generally, if the tangent flow at a singularity
(\mathbf{0},0)
is
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
, and the leading asymptotics of the RMCF is given by a Hermitian polynomial
h
of degree
l\geq 3
with top degree part
q
such that

\mathcal{Z}:=\{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}:\nabla q(\mathbf{y})=0\}\ \ \text{ is not a linear subspace}\,.

(One such example is when
k=2
and
h(y_{1},y_{2})=(y_{1}^{2}-2)(y_{2}^{2}-2)
, in which case
q(y_{1},y_{2})=y_{1}^{2}y_{2}^{2}
and
\mathcal{Z}=\{y_{1}y_{2}=0\}
is a union of two orthogonal lines.) Then, our analysis above doesn’t rule out the possibility that the accumulation set of
k
-cylindrical singularities near
(\mathbf{0},0)
is
\mathcal{Z}
. An interesting question is to construct a MCF whose
2
-cylindrical singular set is a union of curves intersecting at
\mathbf{0}
whose accumulation set is
\{y_{1}y_{2}=0\}
.

6.2.
Closedness of degenerate singular set

Given a
k
-cylindrical singularity
p
, we use
\mathbf{U}_{p}
to denote the arrival time function corresponding to the tangent flow at
p
. Note that
\mathbf{U}_{p}
could possibly be different from
\mathbf{U}_{n,k}
by a rotation. Recall that by the proof of Theorem
1.4
in Section
4.3
,
\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})_{+}
consists of
k
-cylindrical singularities
p
with the limit of decay order
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{U}_{p}}(\infty,\mathcal{M}^{p})>0
. Therefore, the relative closedness of
\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})_{+}
in
\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})
follows directly from the lemma below, asserting that
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{U}_{p}}(\infty,\mathcal{M}^{p})
is upper semi-continuous in
p
.

Proposition 6.5
.

Suppose
\lambda[\mathbf{M}]<+\infty
,
p_{j}\in\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})
,
1\leq j\leq\infty
, and
p_{j}\to p_{\infty}
as
j\to\infty
. Then

\limsup_{j\to\infty}\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{U}_{p_{j}}}(\infty,\mathcal{M}^{p_{j}})\leq\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{U}_{p_{\infty}}}(\infty,\mathcal{M}^{p_{\infty}})\,.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume
p_{\infty}=(\mathbf{0},0)
,
\mathbf{U}_{p_{\infty}}=\mathbf{U}_{n,k}
, and assume
\mathcal{M}
is the associated RMCF with
\lambda[\mathcal{M}]<\varepsilon^{-1}
for some
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. If
\mathcal{N}_{n,k}(\infty,\mathcal{M})=+\infty
then the inequality is automatically true, and in the following we assume
\mathcal{N}_{n,k}(\infty,\mathcal{M})<+\infty
. Then for any
\varepsilon>0
, we can find
T>0
such that for
\tau\geq T
,
|\mathcal{N}_{n,k}(\tau,\mathcal{M})-\mathcal{N}_{n,k}(\infty,\mathcal{M})|<\varepsilon/2
, and
\mathbf{d}_{n,k}(\mathcal{M}(\tau))<\varepsilon/2
. Because
p\to\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{U}_{p}}(\tau,\mathcal{M}^{p})
is continuous for any fixed
\tau
, so does
p\to\mathcal{N}_{n,k}(\tau,\mathcal{M}^{p_{j}})
, and hence we have
|\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{U}_{p_{j}}}(T,\mathcal{M}^{p_{j}})-\mathcal{N}_{n,k}(T,\mathcal{M})|<\varepsilon/2
for sufficiently large
p
. This implies that for any
\varepsilon>0
, there exist
T>0
and
j
sufficiently large, such that
|\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{U}_{p_{j}}}(T,\mathcal{M}^{p_{j}})-\mathcal{N}_{n,k}(\infty,\mathcal{M})|<\varepsilon
, and
\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{U}_{p_{j}}}(\mathcal{M}^{p_{j}}(T))<\varepsilon
. By
[
CM25
, Theorem 0.5]
, if
\varepsilon
is chosen sufficiently small, when
j
is sufficiently large,
\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{U}_{p_{j}}}(\mathcal{M}^{p_{j}}(\tau))
is small for all
\tau\geq T
, and hence Corollary
3.13
can be applied to
\mathcal{M}^{p_{j}}
. As a consequence of the discrete monotonicity formula Corollary
3.13
,
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbf{U}_{p_{j}}}(\infty,\mathcal{M}^{p_{j}})\leq\mathcal{N}_{n,k}(\infty,\mathcal{M})
.
∎

6.3.
A
C^{2,\alpha}
Reifenberg-type Theorem

In this subsection, we use the location estimate in Lemma
5.1
to prove part
(iii)
and
(iv)
of Theorem
1.1
. In fact, we shall prove a general
C^{2,\alpha}
Reifenberg-type result asserting that a compact subset satisfying a reasonable location estimate is locally contained in a
C^{2,\alpha}
submanifold.

We first introduce some notations. Let
\operatorname{Jet}_{n,k}^{2}
be the collection of the data
(\mathbf{L},\mathbf{Q})
, where

•

\mathbf{L}:\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\to\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
is a self-adjoint linear homomorphism given by orthogonal projection onto a
k
-dimensional linear subspace of
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
, denoted by
|\mathbf{L}|
. Let
\mathbf{L}^{\perp}:=\id-\mathbf{L}
be the orthogonal projection onto
|\mathbf{L}|^{\perp}
;

•

\mathbf{Q}:=\mathbf{q}\circ\mathbf{L}
is the quadratic polynomial
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\to|\mathbf{L}|^{\perp}
extended from
\mathbf{q}:|\mathbf{L}|\to|\mathbf{L}|^{\perp}
, a
|\mathbf{L}|^{\perp}
-valued quadratic polynomial on
|\mathbf{L}|
.

Proposition 6.6
.

Let
K\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\times\mathbb{R}
be a compact subset,
\Lambda>0
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Suppose there’s a map
J:K\to\operatorname{Jet}^{2}_{n,k}
satisfying that for every
r\in(0,1]
and every
p_{i}=:(X_{i},t_{i})\in K
with
d_{\mathcal{P}}(p_{1},p_{2})\leq r
and
J(p_{i})=:(\mathbf{L}_{i},\mathbf{Q}_{i})
, we have
\|\mathbf{L}_{1}^{\perp}-\mathbf{L}_{2}^{\perp}\|\leq\Lambda r
;
\|\mathbf{Q}_{1}\|,\,\|\mathbf{Q}_{2}\|\leq\Lambda
, and

(6.6)

\displaystyle\begin{split}r^{-2}|t_{2}-t_{1}|&+r^{-1}|\mathbf{L}_{1}^{\perp}(X_{2}-X_{1})-\mathbf{Q}_{1}(X_{2}-X_{1})|\\
&+\left\|\mathbf{L}_{1}^{\perp}\circ\left((\mathbf{L}_{1}^{\perp}-\mathbf{L}_{2}^{\perp})-\nabla\mathbf{Q}_{1}(X_{2}-X_{1})\right)\right\|+r\|\mathbf{Q}_{2}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}\|\leq\Lambda r^{1+\alpha}\,;\end{split}

where
\|\cdot\|
in the last two terms stands for the operator norm of linear or quadratic
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
-valued maps from
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
. Then,

(i)

K
is locally contained in a
k
-dimensional
C^{2,\alpha}
-submanifold in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\times\mathbb{R}
; and if
K
is a
k
-dimensional submanifold near
p\in K
, then its tangent space is
T_{p}K=\mathbf{L}_{p}
and its second fundamental form at
p
is given by
2\mathbf{q}_{p}
.

(ii)

\dim_{H}\mathfrak{t}(K)\leq k/(3+\alpha)
.

To prove this proposition, recall the following Whitney’s Extension Theorem in
[
Fef09
, Page 5]
.

Lemma 6.7

(Whitney’s Extension Theorem)
.

Let
\alpha\in(0,1]
,
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,
K\subset\mathbb{R}^{N}
be a compact set, and
f:K\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a given mapping.
Suppose that for any
x_{\circ}\in K
there exists a polynomial
P_{x_{\circ}}
such that:

•

P_{x_{\circ}}(x_{\circ})=f(x_{\circ})
;

•

|D^{j}P_{x_{\circ}}(x)-D^{j}P_{x}(x)|\leq\Lambda|x-x_{\circ}|^{\ell+\alpha-j}
for all
x\in K
and
j\in\{0,1,\dots,\ell\}
, where
\Lambda>0
is independent of
x_{\circ}
.

Then there exists
F:\mathbb{R}^{N}\to\mathbb{R}
of class
C^{\ell,\alpha}
such that

F|_{K}\equiv f\qquad\text{and}\qquad F(x)=P_{x_{\circ}}(x)+O(|x-x_{\circ}|^{\ell+\alpha}),\quad\forall\,x_{\circ},x\in K\,.

The basic strategy is then to construct a polynomial from the data
(p,J(p))
and check that it satisfies the second bullet point above.

Proof of Proposition
6.6
.

Associated to every
p_{\circ}=(X_{\circ},t_{\circ})\in K
and
J_{\circ}(p_{\circ})=(\mathbf{L}_{\circ},\mathbf{q}_{\circ})\in\operatorname{Jet}_{n,k}^{2}
, we define a
4
-th order polynomial on
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
by

(6.7)

\mathbf{f}_{p_{\circ}}(X):=t_{\circ}-\left|\mathbf{L}_{\circ}^{\perp}(X-X_{\circ})-\mathbf{Q}_{\circ}(X-X_{\circ})\right|^{2}\,.

Claim.
There exists
\delta_{\Lambda}>0
depending only on
\Lambda
such that for every
p_{1}=(X_{1},t_{1})\in K
and
p_{2}=(X_{2},t_{2})\in K\cap P_{2\delta_{\Lambda}}(p_{1})
, we have
X_{2}\neq X_{1}
and

(6.8)

\displaystyle|D^{j}\mathbf{f}_{p_{1}}(X_{2})-D^{j}\mathbf{f}_{p_{2}}(X_{2})|\lesssim_{n,\Lambda}|X_{2}-X_{1}|^{3+\alpha-j},\qquad\forall\,0\leq j\leq 3\,.

Let’s first finish the proof assuming this Claim. By Lemma
6.7
, for every
p_{1}=(X_{1},t_{1})\in K
, there exists
\mathbf{f}\in C^{3,\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
such that for every
\bar{p}=(\bar{X},\bar{t})\in K\cap P_{\delta_{\Lambda}}(p_{1})
, we have

(6.9)

\displaystyle D^{j}\mathbf{f}(\bar{X})=D^{j}\mathbf{f}_{\bar{p}}(\bar{X}),\qquad\forall\,0\leq j\leq 3\,.

Hence, there’s a neighborhood
U_{1}\subset B^{n+1}_{\delta_{\Lambda}}(X_{1})
of
X_{1}
such that
\nabla_{\mathbf{L}_{1}^{\perp}}\mathbf{f}:U_{1}\to\mathbb{R}^{n-k+1}
is a
C^{2,\alpha}
map with non-degenerate differential at
X_{1}
. Thus
\breve{\Gamma}:=\{X\in U_{1}:\nabla_{\mathbf{L}_{1}^{\perp}}\mathbf{f}(X)=0\}
is a
k
-dimensional
C^{2,\alpha}
-submanifold of
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
. Also, by taking
j=0,1
in (
6.9
), it’s easy to check that

K\cap(U_{1}\times[t_{\circ}-\delta_{\Lambda}^{2},t_{\circ}+\delta_{\Lambda}^{2}])\subset\{(\bar{X},\mathbf{f}(\bar{X})):\bar{X}\in U_{1}\cap\breve{\Gamma}\}=:\Gamma\,,

where
\Gamma
is the graph of a
C^{3,\alpha}
function over a
C^{2,\alpha}
submanifold
\breve{\Gamma}
in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\times\mathbb{R}
, hence is a
k
-dimensional
C^{2,\alpha}
-submanifold in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\times\mathbb{R}
. Now suppose
K
is a submanifold, and without loss of generality, assume
X_{1}=\mathbf{0}
,
\mathbf{L}_{1}=\{(0,y):y\in\mathbb{R}^{k}\}
, we may assume
G:\mathbb{R}^{k}\to\mathbb{R}^{n-k+1}
is locally defined such that
\nabla_{\mathbf{L}_{1}^{\perp}}\mathbf{f}(G(y),y)=0
in a neighborhood of
\mathbf{0}
. Then, by direct calculations, at
y=0
, we have

(6.10)

\displaystyle\nabla_{y}G=\nabla_{y}\mathbf{q},

\displaystyle\quad\nabla^{2}_{y}G=\nabla^{2}_{y}\mathbf{q}=2\mathbf{q}.

This shows the criterion for the second fundamental form of
K
.

Moreover, the estimate (
6.6
) on
|t_{2}-t_{1}|
term implies that
\mathfrak{t}|_{K\cap P_{\delta_{\Lambda}}(p_{1})}
is
(3+\alpha)
-Hölder. The Hausdorff dimension estimate of
\mathfrak{t}(K)
then follows by a standard covering argument, see
[
FROS20
, Proposition 7.7]
.

Proof of the Claim.

Let us do the Taylor expansion of
(\mathbf{f}_{p_{1}}-\mathbf{f}_{p_{2}})
at
X_{2}
. Since this a polynomial, it suffices to rewrite
(\mathbf{f}_{p_{1}}-\mathbf{f}_{p_{2}})(X_{2}+X)
into sum of homogeneous polynomials in
X
:

\displaystyle(\mathbf{f}_{p_{1}}-\mathbf{f}_{p_{2}})(X_{2}+X)

\displaystyle\quad=(t_{1}-t_{2})+|\mathbf{L}_{2}^{\perp}(X)-\mathbf{Q}_{2}(X)|^{2}-\left|\mathbf{L}_{1}^{\perp}((X_{2}-X_{1})+X)-\mathbf{Q}_{1}((X_{2}-X_{1})+X)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad=(t_{1}-t_{2})+|\mathbf{L}_{2}^{\perp}(X)-\mathbf{Q}_{2}(X)|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\ -\Big|\underbrace{\left(\mathbf{L}_{1}^{\perp}(X_{2}-X_{1})-\mathbf{Q}_{1}(X_{2}-X_{1})\right)}_{=:\,a}+\underbrace{\left(\mathbf{L}_{1}^{\perp}(X)-\nabla_{X}\mathbf{Q}_{1}(X_{2}-X_{1})\right)}_{=:\,b(X)}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}(X)\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad=:\mathcal{T}_{0}(X)+\mathcal{T}_{1}(X)+\mathcal{T}_{2}(X)+\mathcal{T}_{3}(X)+\mathcal{T}_{4}(X)\,;

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{0}(X)

\displaystyle=(t_{1}-t_{2})-|a|^{2}\,;

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{1}(X)

\displaystyle=-2\left\langle a,\ b(X)\right\rangle\,;

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{2}(X)

\displaystyle=|\mathbf{L}_{2}^{\perp}(X)|^{2}-|b(X)|^{2}+2\left\langle a,\ \mathbf{Q}_{1}(X)\right\rangle\,;

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{3}(X)

\displaystyle=-2\langle\mathbf{L}_{2}^{\perp}(X),\ \mathbf{Q}_{2}(X)\rangle+2\langle b(X),\ \mathbf{Q}_{1}(X)\rangle\,;

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{4}(X)

\displaystyle=|\mathbf{Q}_{2}(X)|^{2}-|\mathbf{Q}_{1}(X)|^{2}\,.

If we set
r:=d_{\mathcal{P}}(p_{1},p_{2})
, then by (
6.6
), when
r\leq 2\delta_{\Lambda}\ll 1
, we have

\displaystyle|X_{2}-X_{1}|\sim_{n}r\,,

\displaystyle|a|\lesssim_{n,\Lambda}r^{2+\alpha}\,,

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{L}_{1}^{\perp}\left(b(X)-\mathbf{L}_{2}^{\perp}(X)\right)\right|\lesssim_{n,\Lambda}r^{1+\alpha}|X|\,.

Also note that
(\mathbf{L}_{1}^{\perp})^{2}=\mathbf{L}_{1}^{\perp}
,
\mathbf{L}_{1}^{\perp}\circ\mathbf{Q}_{1}=\mathbf{Q}_{1}
, and

\displaystyle|\mathbf{L}_{i}^{\perp}(X)|\lesssim_{n}|X|,

\displaystyle|(\mathbf{L}_{1}^{\perp}-\mathbf{L}_{2}^{\perp})(X)|+|\nabla_{X}\mathbf{Q}_{1}(X_{2}-X_{1})|\lesssim_{n,\Lambda}r|X|,

\displaystyle|\mathbf{Q}_{i}(X)|\lesssim_{n,\Lambda}|X|^{2}\,.

Hence (
6.6
) and estimates above imply that for every
X\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
and every
0\leq j\leq 3
,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{T}_{j}(X)|\lesssim_{n,\Lambda}r^{3+\alpha-j}|X|^{j}\,.

While since
\mathcal{T}_{j}
is the
j
-th order Taylor polynomial of
(\mathbf{f}_{p_{1}}-\mathbf{f}_{p_{2}})
at
X_{2}
, we then have

|D^{j}(\mathbf{f}_{p_{1}}-\mathbf{f}_{p_{2}})(X_{2})|\sim_{n,j}\|\mathcal{T}_{j}\|_{C^{0}(B_{1}^{n+1})}\lesssim_{n,\Lambda}r^{3+\alpha-j}\,.

∎

∎

Proof of Theorem
1.1

(iii)
and
(iv)
.

Let
\alpha\in(0,\min\{1,\frac{2}{n-k}\})
and we choose
\varepsilon>0
smaller such that
\gamma^{+}_{n,k}-10\varepsilon\geq\alpha
. For every
p\in\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})_{+}
, we first claim that there exists
s_{p}>0
such that for every
r\in(0,s_{p}]
,
\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})\cap\overline{P_{r}(p)}
is compact (note that by item
(ii)
proved in Section
6.2
, this implies the compactness of
\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})_{+}\cap\overline{P_{r}(p)}
). Suppose for contradiction that there exist sequences of radius
s_{j}\searrow 0
and limit points
p_{j}\in\overline{P_{s_{j}}(p)}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})
of
k
-cylindrical singularities. Then by the upper semi-continuity of the Gaussian density, we have

\Theta_{\mathbf{M}}(p_{j})\geq\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{C}_{n,k}]=\Theta_{\mathbf{M}}(p),\quad\forall\,j\geq 1\,;\qquad\liminf_{j\to\infty}\Theta_{\mathbf{M}}(p_{j})\leq\Theta_{\mathbf{M}}(p)\,.

Hence as
j\to\infty
,
\Theta_{\mathbf{M}}(p_{j})\to\Theta_{\mathbf{M}}(p)
, and since the tangent flow of
\mathbf{M}
at
p
is a rotation of round cylinder
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
with multiplicity
1
, from the rigidity of round cylinders by Colding-Ilmanen-Minicozzi
[
CIM15
]
and the quantitative uniqueness of tangent flow by Colding-Minicozzi
[
CM25
]
, when
j\gg 1
, the tangent flow of
\mathbf{M}
at
p_{j}
is also a rotation of
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
. This contradicts the assumption
p_{j}\notin\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})
.

Now for every
p\in\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})_{+}
, again by the quantitative uniqueness of tangent flow by Colding-Minicozzi
[
CM25
]
, we can take
r_{p}\in(0,s_{p}]
such that for every
p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})_{+}\cap\overline{P_{r_{p}}(p)}
, after a rotation, the RMCF of
r_{p}^{-1}\cdot(\mathbf{M}-p^{\prime})
is
\delta_{\ref{Lem_C^2 Reg_Main}}(n,\varepsilon)
-
L^{2}
close to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
over
\tau\in[-1,+\infty)
, and converges to
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
as
\tau\to+\infty
. By an a priori rescaling of
\mathbf{M}
, we may assume without loss of generality that
r_{p}=1
. We shall conclude item
(iii)
by applying Proposition
6.6
to
K:=\mathcal{S}_{k}(\mathbf{M})_{+}\cap\overline{P_{\delta_{\ref{Lem_C^2 Reg_Main}}(n,\varepsilon)}(p)}
, where to each
p\in K
, the Whitney data assigned to
p
is
(\mathbf{L}_{p},\mathbf{Q}_{p})
as specified at the beginning of Section
5
. For every
p_{1},p_{2}\in K
, the upper bound on
\mathbf{Q}_{i}
holds by Lemma
5.1
, hence it suffices to verify (
6.6
). By a translation and rotation, we assume without loss of generality that
p_{1}=(\mathbf{0},0)
and
\mathbf{L}_{1}=\{0\}\times\mathbb{R}^{k}
. Hence by writing
p_{2}=(x_{2},y_{2},t_{2})
and applying (
5.2
) with
\mathbf{L}_{1},\mathbf{q}_{1},p_{2},\mathbf{L}_{2},\mathbf{q}_{2}
in place of
\mathbf{L}_{\circ},\mathbf{q}_{\circ},\bar{p},\bar{\mathbf{L}},\bar{\mathbf{q}}
, we get desired estimates on terms
|t_{2}-t_{1}|
,
|\mathbf{L}_{1}^{\perp}(X_{2}-X_{1})-\mathbf{Q}_{1}(X_{2}-X_{1})|
and
|\mathbf{Q}_{2}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}|
. We also derive that for some
\bar{\mathbf{A}}=\begin{bmatrix}0&\bar{\mathbf{\ell}}\\
-\bar{\mathbf{\ell}}^{\top}&0\end{bmatrix}
satisfying
\|\bar{\mathbf{\ell}}\|\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}r
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{L}_{2}^{\perp}=e^{\mathbf{A}}\circ\mathbf{L}_{1}^{\perp}\circ e^{-\mathbf{A}}=(I_{n}+\mathbf{A}+O(|\mathbf{A}|^{2}))\circ\mathbf{L}_{1}^{\perp}\circ(I_{n}-\mathbf{A}+O(|\mathbf{A}|^{2}))\,,

hence

\left|\mathbf{L}_{1}^{\perp}-\mathbf{L}_{2}^{\perp}-\begin{bmatrix}0&\bar{\mathbf{\ell}}\\
\bar{\mathbf{\ell}}^{\top}&0\end{bmatrix}\right|\lesssim_{n}|\bar{\mathbf{A}}|^{2}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}r^{2}

While since
\nabla\mathbf{Q}_{1}(X_{2}-X_{1})=\begin{bmatrix}0&\nabla\mathbf{q}_{1}(y_{2})\\
0&0\end{bmatrix}
, we thus conclude the estimates on
\|\mathbf{L}_{1}^{\perp}-\mathbf{L}_{2}^{\perp}\|
and
\left\|\mathbf{L}_{1}^{\perp}\circ\left((\mathbf{L}_{1}^{\perp}-\mathbf{L}_{2}^{\perp})-\nabla\mathbf{Q}_{1}(X_{2}-X_{1})\right)\right\|
from these estimates above and (
5.2
) again.

Finally, item
(iv)
follows from item
(ii)
by letting
\alpha
approach
\min\{1,\frac{2}{n-k}\}
.
∎

Appendix A
A family of convex mean curvature flow with slowest asymptotics

In this section, we will construct rescaled mean curvature flows that are asymptotic to the sphere with prescribed decay. Such flows have been constructed in
[
Ses08
,
Str20
]
, but we need a variant version for our purpose. Let us first recall some of the results by Strehlke from
[
Str20
]
. For
m\geq 1
,
\mathbb{S}^{m}:=S^{m}(\sqrt{2m})\subset\mathbb{R}^{m+1}
, a rescaled mean curvature flow as a graph of a function
(u(\tau))_{\tau\geq 0}
over it satisfies the equation

(A.1)

\partial_{\tau}u=L_{\mathbb{S}^{m}}u+\mathscr{N}(u,\nabla u,\nabla^{2}u),

where
\mathscr{N}(u,\nabla u,\nabla^{2}u)
is a quadratic nonlinear term which can be written as
f(u,\nabla u)+\tr(B(u,\nabla u)\nabla^{2}u)
for some smooth function
f,B
with
f(0,0)=0
,
df(0,0)=0
and
B(0,0)=0
. For simplicity, we write
\mathscr{N}(u):=\mathscr{N}(u,\nabla u,\nabla^{2}u)
.

-L_{\mathbb{S}^{m}}:=-(\Delta_{\mathbb{S}^{m}}+1)
has eigenvalues
\frac{i(i+m-1)}{2m}-1
for
i=0,1,2,\cdots
, and first several eigenvalues are
\lambda_{1}=-1
,
\lambda_{2}=-\frac{1}{2}
,
\lambda_{3}=\frac{1}{m}
, and
\lambda_{4}=\frac{m+6}{2m}
. Let
\mathfrak{X}_{m}
be the space of
L^{2}
-eigenfunctions of the
-L_{\mathbb{S}^{m}}
with eigenvalue
1/m
. We use
\|\psi\|
to denote the
L^{2}
-norm of
\psi\in\mathfrak{X}_{m}
, and use
B^{\mathfrak{X}_{m}}_{r}
to denote the closed ball of radius
r
in
(\mathfrak{X}_{m},\|\cdot\|)
centered at
0
. Note that since
\mathfrak{X}_{m}
is a finite-dimensional vector space, all the norms are equivalent over
\mathfrak{X}_{m}
.

Let us state the main results of this section.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
m\geq 1
,
\mathfrak{X}_{m}
be defined as above. Then for any
\sigma\in(1/m,2/m)
, there exist a
\varkappa=\varkappa(m,\sigma)>0
and a
C^{1}
map
\Xi:B_{\varkappa}^{\mathfrak{X}_{m}}\to C^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0})
such that

(i)

\Xi(0)=0
;

(ii)

for every
\psi\in B_{\varkappa}^{\mathfrak{X}_{m}}
,
w:=\Xi(\psi)
satisfies
\|w\|_{C^{2}}\leq 1
and
\tau\mapsto\mathrm{graph}_{\mathbb{S}^{m}}(w(\cdot,\tau))
forms a rescaled mean curvature flow.

(iii)

for every
\varkappa^{\prime}\in(0,\varkappa]
and
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in B_{\varkappa^{\prime}}^{\mathfrak{X}_{m}}
, let
w_{i}:=\Xi(\psi_{i})
,
i=1,2
. Then we have,

\|(w_{1}-w_{2})(\cdot,\tau)-e^{-\frac{\tau}{m}}(\psi_{1}-\psi_{2})\|_{C^{4}(\mathbb{S}^{m})}\lesssim_{m,\sigma}\varkappa^{\prime}e^{-\sigma\tau}\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{m})},\qquad\forall\,\tau\geq 0\,.

Throughout this section, every constant in our estimate is also allowed to depend on the dimension
m
of the sphere. For notational simplicity, we shall not indicate it in the statements.

A.1.
Estimates of the nonlinearity and the solution

The following Lemma of Strehlke
[
Str20
]
estimates the nonlinear term
\mathscr{N}
in the equation.

Lemma A.2

(
[
Str20
, Lemma 3.5]
)
.

For
\ell>m/2+1
and
u,v\in H^{\ell}(\mathbb{S}^{m})
with
\|u\|_{H^{\ell}}\leq 1
,
\|v\|_{H^{\ell}}\leq 1
,

(A.2)

\begin{split}\|\mathscr{N}(u)-\mathscr{N}(v)\|_{H^{\ell-1}}\lesssim_{\ell}(\|u\|_{H^{\ell+1}}\|u-v\|_{H^{\ell}}+\|u\|_{H^{\ell}}\|u-v\|_{H^{\ell+1}}).\end{split}

Now let us fix
1/m=\lambda_{3}<\eta<\sigma<2/m<\lambda_{4}
. For any integer
\ell>0
, we use
\mathfrak{Y}_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}
to denote the space of functions
u:[0,\infty)\to H^{\ell+1}(\mathbb{S}^{m})
such that

(A.3)

\|u\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}:=\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{2\eta\tau}\|u(\tau)\|_{H^{\ell+1}(\mathbb{S}^{m})}^{2}\ d\tau\right)^{1/2}+\sup_{\tau\geq 0}e^{\sigma\tau}\|u(\tau)\|_{H^{\ell}(\mathbb{S}^{m})}<+\infty.

Note that our defined norm is slightly different from
[
Str20
]
because we need to treat different decay rates. We use
\Pi:L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{m})\to L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{m})
to denote the
L^{2}
-orthogonal projection onto the space of eigenfunctions with eigenvalue
\geq\lambda_{4}
. We need the following two estimates that are variant versions of Corollaries 3.3 and 3.4 in
[
Str20
]
.

Lemma A.3
.

If
v(s)
is a continuously differentiable path in
H^{\ell}
with
\Pi v(s)=v(s)
for all
s\geq 0
, then for
1/m<\eta<\sigma<2/m
and integer
\ell\geq 1
,

(A.4)

e^{2\sigma\tau}\|v(\tau)\|_{H^{\ell}}^{2}\leq\|v(0)\|_{H^{\ell}}^{2}+\frac{\lambda_{4}}{2(\lambda_{4}-\sigma)}\int_{0}^{\tau}e^{2\sigma s}\|(\partial_{s}-L)v(s)\|_{H^{\ell-1}}^{2}\ ds,

and if there is a sequence of
\tau_{j}\to\infty
such that
e^{\eta\tau}\|v(\tau_{j})\|_{H^{\ell}}\to 0
, then

(A.5)

\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{2\eta\tau}\|v(\tau)\|_{H^{\ell+1}}^{2}\ d\tau\lesssim_{\ell,\eta}\|v(0)\|_{H^{\ell}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{2\eta\tau}\|(\partial_{\tau}-L)v(\tau)\|_{H^{\ell-1}}^{2}\ d\tau.

The proof is essentially the same as Corollaries 3.3 and 3.4 in
[
Str20
]
, based on Lemma 3.2 therein, so we skip the proof here.

For any
(u,\phi)\in\mathfrak{Y}_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}\times\mathfrak{X}_{m}
with
\|u\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}+\|\phi\|_{H^{\ell}}\leq 1
, we define
U(\cdot;u,\phi)\in\mathfrak{Y}_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}
to be the unique solution to the equation

(A.6)

\begin{cases}(\partial_{\tau}-L)U(\cdot;u,\phi)=\mathscr{N}(u+e^{-\frac{\tau}{m}}\phi),\\
\Pi\ U(\cdot,0;u,\phi)=0.\end{cases}

The existence of the solution in
\mathfrak{Y}_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}
is a direct consequence of the semigroup argument together with Lemma
A.2
. In fact, we have an explicit expression of
U
:

(A.7)

U(\cdot,\tau;u,\phi)=\int_{0}^{\tau}e^{(\tau-s)L}\Pi\mathscr{N}(u+e^{-\frac{s}{m}}\phi)\ ds-\int_{\tau}^{\infty}e^{(\tau-s)L}(1-\Pi)\mathscr{N}(u+e^{-\frac{s}{m}}\phi)\ ds\,,

where
e^{\tau L}
denotes the heat semi-group for
L=L_{\mathbb{S}^{m}}
, and we can write down
U(0;u,\phi)
explicitly:

U(\cdot,0;u,\phi)=-\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-sL}(1-\Pi)\mathscr{N}(u+e^{-\frac{s}{m}}\phi)\ ds.

It follows directly from (
A.7
) that
U(\cdot;0,0)=0
. The following stronger estimate for
U
allows us to conclude that
U(\cdot,u,\phi)\in\mathfrak{Y}_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}
by taking
(v,\psi)=(0,0)
therein.

Lemma A.4
.

For
\ell>m/2+1
, there exist
r_{\ref{Lem:AppCContraction}}=r_{\ref{Lem:AppCContraction}}(\ell,\sigma,\eta)>0
such that when
u,v\in\mathfrak{Y}_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}
and
\phi,\psi\in\mathfrak{X}_{m}
with
(\|u\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}+\|v\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}+\|\phi\|+\|\psi\|)\leq r_{\ref{Lem:AppCContraction}}
, we have

(A.8)

\|U(\cdot;u,\phi)-U(\cdot;v,\psi)\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}\lesssim_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}(\|u\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}+\|v\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}+\|\phi\|+\|\psi\|)(\|u-v\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}+\|\phi-\psi\|)\,.

Proof.

Let
D(\tau)=U(\cdot,\tau;u,\phi)-U(\cdot,\tau;v,\psi)
, then
D
satisfies the equations

\begin{split}(\partial_{\tau}-L)D&=\mathscr{N}(u+e^{-\frac{\tau}{m}}\phi)-\mathscr{N}(v+e^{-\frac{\tau}{m}}\psi)\,,\\
D(\cdot,0)&=-\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-Ls}(1-\Pi)\big(\mathscr{N}(u+e^{-\frac{\tau}{m}}\phi)-\mathscr{N}(v+e^{-\frac{\tau}{m}}\psi)\big)\ ds\,.\end{split}

Then the proof is similar to the proof of Theorem 3.7 in
[
Str20
]
5
5

5

In
[
Str20
]
, it was labeled as “proof of Theorem 3.3”, and it seems to be a typo.
, but we need to single out the terms
e^{-\frac{\tau}{m}}\phi
and
e^{-\frac{\tau}{m}}\psi
, as they are not in
\mathfrak{Y}_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}
.
For the readers’ convenience, we sketch the proof as follows. From the expression of
D
,

\begin{split}D(\tau)=&\,\int_{0}^{\tau}e^{(\tau-s)L}\Pi\big(\mathscr{N}(u+e^{-\frac{s}{m}}\phi)-\mathscr{N}(v+e^{-\frac{s}{m}}\psi)\big)\ ds\\
&-\int_{\tau}^{\infty}e^{(\tau-s)L}(1-\Pi)\big(\mathscr{N}(u+e^{-\frac{s}{m}}\phi)-\mathscr{N}(v+e^{-\frac{s}{m}}\psi)\big)\ ds\,.\end{split}

When
\tau\geq s
,
\|e^{(\tau-s)L}\|\leq e^{-\lambda_{4}(\tau-s)}\leq e^{-\frac{2}{m}(\tau-s)}
on
\Pi(H^{\ell})
, and when
\tau\leq s
,
\|e^{(\tau-s)L}\|\leq e^{-\frac{1}{m}(\tau-s)}=e^{\frac{1}{m}(s-\tau)}
on
(1-\Pi)(H^{\ell})
.
Now we estimate
\|(1-\Pi)D(\tau)\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}
and
\|\Pi D(\tau)\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}
separately.

\bullet
Estimate of
\|(1-\Pi)D(\tau)\|_{{\ell,\sigma,\eta}}
.
By the expression of the solution,

(1-\Pi)D(\tau)=-\int_{\tau}^{\infty}e^{(\tau-s)L}(1-\Pi)\big(\mathscr{N}(u+e^{-\frac{s}{m}}\phi)-\mathscr{N}(v+e^{-\frac{s}{m}}\psi)\big)ds.

Therefore, if we denote for simplicity
\phi_{s}:=e^{-\frac{s}{m}}\phi
and
\psi_{s}:=e^{-\frac{s}{m}}\psi
, then

(A.9)

\begin{split}e^{\sigma\tau}\|(1-\Pi)D(\tau)\|_{H^{\ell}}&\lesssim_{\ell}\int_{\tau}^{\infty}e^{\frac{1}{m}(s-\tau)+\sigma\tau}\|(1-\Pi)\big(\mathscr{N}(u+\phi_{s})-\mathscr{N}(v+\psi_{s})\big)\|_{H^{\ell-1}}\ ds\\
&\lesssim_{\ell}\int_{\tau}^{\infty}e^{\frac{1}{m}(s-\tau)+\sigma\tau}\|\mathscr{N}(u+\phi_{s})-\mathscr{N}(v+\psi_{s})\|_{H^{\ell-1}}ds.\end{split}

Here we use the fact that
(1-\Pi)
is a finite rank operator and all the norms are equivalent. Then by Lemma
A.2
and triangle inquality,

\begin{split}&\int_{\tau}^{\infty}e^{\frac{1}{m}(s-\tau)+\sigma\tau}\|\mathscr{N}(u+\phi_{s})-\mathscr{N}(v+\psi_{s})\|_{H^{\ell-1}}ds\\
&\quad\lesssim_{\ell}\int_{\tau}^{\infty}e^{\frac{1}{m}(s-\tau)+\sigma\tau}\Big((\|u(s)\|_{H^{\ell+1}}+e^{-\frac{s}{m}}\|\phi\|)\cdot(\|u(s)-v(s)\|_{H^{\ell}}+e^{-\frac{s}{m}}\|\phi-\psi\|)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\;\;\;+(\|v(s)\|_{H^{\ell}}+e^{-\frac{s}{m}}\|\psi\|)\cdot(\|u(s)-v(s)\|_{H^{\ell+1}}+e^{-\frac{s}{m}}\|\phi-\psi\|)\Big)\ ds.\end{split}

We can bound it by
(\|u\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}+\|v\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}+\|\phi\|+\|\psi\|)(\|u-v\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}+\|\phi-\psi\|)
. For example:

\begin{split}\int_{\tau}^{\infty}e^{\frac{1}{m}(s-\tau)+\sigma\tau}\|u(s)\|_{H^{\ell+1}}\|u(s)-v(s)\|_{H^{\ell}}\ ds\leq\|u(s)-v(s)\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}\int_{\tau}^{\infty}e^{(\frac{1}{m}-\sigma)(s-\tau)}\|u(s)\|_{H^{\ell+1}}\ ds\,,\end{split}

and by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\begin{split}\int_{\tau}^{\infty}e^{(\frac{1}{m}-\sigma)(s-\tau)}\|u(s)\|_{H^{\ell+1}}\ ds&\leq\left(\int_{\tau}^{\infty}e^{2(\frac{1}{m}-\sigma)(s-\tau)-2\eta s}\ ds\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{\tau}^{\infty}e^{2\eta s}\|u(s)\|^{2}_{H^{\ell+1}}\ ds\right)^{1/2}\\
&\lesssim_{\sigma,\eta}\|u(s)\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}.\end{split}

Another term can be estimated as follows:

\begin{split}\int_{\tau}^{\infty}e^{\frac{1}{m}(s-\tau)+\sigma\tau}\|u(s)\|_{H^{\ell+1}}\cdot e^{-\frac{s}{m}}\|\phi-\psi\|_{H^{\ell}}\ ds\lesssim_{\ell}\|\phi-\psi\|\int_{\tau}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{1}{m}\tau+\sigma\tau}\|u(s)\|_{H^{\ell+1}}\ ds\,,\end{split}

and similarly, Cauchy-Schwarz inequality yields the desired estimate. All the rest terms can be estimated similarly.

Once we bound
e^{\sigma\tau}\|(1-\Pi)D(\tau)\|_{H^{\ell}}
as above, because
(1-\Pi)
has finite rank, we also have

e^{\eta\tau}\|(1-\Pi)D(\tau)\|_{H^{\ell+1}}\lesssim_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}e^{-(\sigma-\eta)\tau}(\|u\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}+\|v\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}+\|\phi\|+\|\psi\|)(\|u-v\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}+\|\phi-\psi\|)\,,

for every
\tau\geq 0
. Integrating over
(0,+\infty)
and using
\eta<\sigma
show that

\|(1-\Pi)D\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}\lesssim_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}(\|u\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}+\|v\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}+\|\phi\|+\|\psi\|)(\|u-v\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}+\|\phi-\psi\|).

\bullet
Estimate of
\|\Pi D(\tau)\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}
.
We observe that
\Pi D(0)=0
. Then by (
A.4
) of Lemma
A.3
,

\begin{split}e^{2\sigma\tau}\|\Pi D(\tau)\|_{H^{\ell}}^{2}&\leq\frac{\lambda_{4}}{2(\lambda_{4}-\sigma)}\int_{0}^{\tau}e^{2\sigma s}\left\|\Pi\big(\mathscr{N}(u+e^{-\frac{s}{m}}\phi)-\mathscr{N}(v+e^{-\frac{s}{m}}\psi)\big)\right\|^{2}_{H^{\ell-1}}ds\\
&\leq\frac{\lambda_{4}}{2(\lambda_{4}-\sigma)}\int_{0}^{\tau}e^{2\sigma s}\left\|\mathscr{N}(u+e^{-\frac{s}{m}}\phi)-\mathscr{N}(v+e^{-\frac{s}{m}}\psi)\right\|^{2}_{H^{\ell-1}}ds.\end{split}

Then Lemma
A.2
shows that

\begin{split}e^{2\sigma\tau}\|\Pi D(\tau)\|_{H^{\ell}}^{2}&\lesssim_{\ell,\sigma}\int_{0}^{\tau}e^{2\sigma s}\Big(\|u+e^{-\frac{s}{m}}\phi\|^{2}_{H^{\ell+1}}\cdot\|(u-v)+e^{-\frac{s}{m}}(\phi-\psi)\|^{2}_{H^{\ell}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\quad+\|v+e^{-\frac{s}{m}}\psi\|^{2}_{H^{\ell}}\cdot\|(u-v)+e^{-\frac{s}{m}}(\phi-\psi)\|^{2}_{H^{\ell+1}}\Big)\ ds\,.\end{split}

which is bounded by

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{2\sigma s}\Big((\|u\|^{2}_{H^{\ell+1}}+e^{-\frac{2s}{m}}\|\phi\|^{2})\cdot(\|u-v\|^{2}_{H^{\ell}}+e^{-\frac{2s}{m}}\|\phi-\psi\|^{2})\\
\ &\qquad\quad\;+(\|v\|^{2}_{H^{\ell}}+e^{-\frac{2s}{m}}\|\psi\|^{2})\cdot(\|u-v\|^{2}_{H^{\ell+1}}+e^{-\frac{2s}{m}}\|\phi-\psi\|^{2})\Big)\ ds\\
&\lesssim_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}(\|u\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}+\|v\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}+\|\phi\|+\|\psi\|)^{2}(\|u-v\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}+\|\phi-\psi\|)^{2}\,.\end{split}

Here we use the fact that
e^{2\sigma s}=e^{2\eta s}e^{2(\sigma-\eta)s}
, and
\sigma-\eta-1/m<\sigma-2/m<0
.

Similarly, by (
A.5
) of Lemma
A.3
,

\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{2\eta\tau}\|\Pi D(\tau)\|_{H^{\ell+1}}^{2}\ d\tau\lesssim_{\ell,\eta}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{2\eta\tau}\|\mathscr{N}(u+e^{-\frac{s}{m}}\phi)-\mathscr{N}(v+e^{-\frac{s}{m}}\psi)\|_{H^{\ell-1}}^{2}\ d\tau.

Because
e^{2\eta\tau}\leq e^{2\sigma\tau}
when
\tau\geq 0
, we obtain similar bound as above. Therefore, we have

\|\Pi D\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}\lesssim_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}(\|u\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}+\|v\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}+\|\phi\|+\|\psi\|)(\|u-v\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}+\|\phi-\psi\|)\,.

Combining the estimates of
\|(1-\Pi)D\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}
and
\|\Pi D\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}
above gives the desired inequality.
∎

A.2.
Proof of Lemma
A.1

Proof.

We first construct the map
\Xi
. Fix a sufficiently large integer
\ell=\ell(m)
so that
H^{\ell}(\mathbb{S}^{m})
is embedded in
C^{4}(\mathbb{S}^{m})
; and fix
1/m<\eta<\sigma<2/m
. Let
\mathfrak{P}:=\mathfrak{Y}_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}\times\mathfrak{X}_{n}
, equipped with the norm
\|(u,\phi)\|_{\mathfrak{P}}=\|u\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}+\|\phi\|
. We define a map
\mathfrak{T}:\mathfrak{P}\to\mathfrak{P}
by

(A.10)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}(u,\phi):=(U(\cdot;u,\phi),0)\,.

Claim.

There exist
0<\varkappa<\kappa<r_{\ref{Lem:AppCContraction}}(\ell,\sigma,\eta)
such that
\id-\mathfrak{T}
is a
C^{1}
diffeomorphism from a subdomain of
B^{\mathfrak{P}}_{\kappa}
onto
B_{\varkappa}^{\mathfrak{P}}
.

Proof of the Claim.

First note that
\id-\mathfrak{T}
is
C^{1}
from the explicit expression of
U
in (
A.7
). Also by Lemma
A.4
, for every
(u_{i},\phi_{i})\in\mathfrak{P}
with
\|(u_{i},\phi_{i})\|_{\mathfrak{P}}\leq r_{\ref{Lem:AppCContraction}}/2
, we have

(A.11)

\displaystyle\|U(\tau;u_{1},\phi_{1})-U(\tau;u_{2},\phi_{2})\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}\leq\bar{C}(\|(u_{1},\phi_{1})\|_{\mathfrak{P}}+\|(u_{2},\phi_{2})\|_{\mathfrak{P}})\cdot\|(u_{1},\phi_{1})-(u_{2},\phi_{2})\|_{\mathfrak{P}}\,.

where
\bar{C}=\bar{C}(\ell,\sigma,\eta)
. Therefore, by fixing the choice of
\varkappa\in(0,\min\{r_{\ref{Lem:AppCContraction}}/2,1/(8\bar{C})\})
and
\kappa=2\varkappa
, we have
D(\id-\mathfrak{T})
is invertible at every point in
B^{\mathfrak{P}}_{\kappa}
.

We are left to show that for every
(v,\psi)\in B^{\mathfrak{P}}_{\varkappa}
, there’s a unique element in
B^{\mathfrak{P}}_{\kappa}\cap(\id-\mathfrak{T})^{-1}(v,\psi)
. To see this, we define a map

\mathfrak{F}_{v,\psi}:\mathfrak{P}\to\mathfrak{P},\quad\mathfrak{F}(u,\phi):=(v,\psi)+\mathfrak{T}(u,\phi)\,.

By the choice of
\kappa,\varkappa
above (and recall that
U(\cdot;0,0)=0
), one can easily check that
\mathfrak{F}_{v,\psi}
is a contraction map from
B^{\mathfrak{P}}_{\kappa}
to itself, and thus has a unique fixed point
(\bar{v},\bar{\psi})\in B^{\mathfrak{P}}_{\kappa}
. This shows that
B^{\mathfrak{P}}_{\kappa}\cap(\id-\mathfrak{T})^{-1}(v,\psi)=\{(\bar{v},\bar{\psi})\}
consists of a single element.
∎

Now for every
\psi\in\mathfrak{X}_{m}
with
\|\psi\|\leq\varkappa
, we define

\Xi(\psi)=\xi(\psi)+e^{-\frac{\tau}{m}}\psi\,,

where
(\xi(\psi),\psi):=(\id-\mathfrak{T})^{-1}(0,\psi)
. Also by the Claim,
\Xi
is a
C^{1}
-map into
\mathfrak{Y}_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}
. Let us verify that
\Xi
satisfies all the items in Lemma
A.1
.

Item
(i)
: Because
(\id-\mathfrak{T})(0,0)=(0,0)
, we have
(\xi(0),0)=(\id-\mathfrak{T})^{-1}(0,0)=(0,0)
. Thus
\Xi(0)=\xi(0)+0=0
.

Item
(ii)
: Since by (
A.10
),

(0,\psi)=(\id-\mathfrak{T})(\xi(\psi),\psi)=(\xi(\psi)-U(\cdot;\xi(\psi),\psi),\psi)\,,

we see that
\Xi(\psi)=U(\cdot;\xi(\psi);\psi)+e^{-\frac{\tau}{m}}\psi
satisfies

(\partial_{\tau}-L)\Xi(\psi)=(\partial_{\tau}-L)U(\cdot;\xi(\psi);\psi)=\mathscr{N}(\xi(\psi)+e^{-\frac{\tau}{m}}\psi)=\mathscr{N}(\Xi(\psi))\,,

where the second equality follows from (
A.6
). Thus
\Xi(\psi)
solves the rescaled mean curvature flow equation (
A.1
). By the choice of
\ell
and the Sobolev embedding theorem,
\mathfrak{Y}_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}
is embedded in
L^{\infty}((0,+\infty),C^{4}(\mathbb{S}^{m}))
. Hence the fact that
\Xi(\psi)\in\mathfrak{Y}_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}
solves the rescaled mean curvature flow equation implies that
\Xi
is a
C^{1}
-map into
C^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0})
as well; and when
\varkappa=\varkappa(m,\sigma)
is chosen sufficiently small, by Sobolev embedding,
\|\Xi(\psi)\|_{C^{2}}\leq\|\xi(\psi)\|_{C^{2}}+\|e^{-\frac{\tau}{m}}\psi\|_{C^{2}}\leq 1
.

Item
(iii)
: Let
w_{i}=\Xi(\psi_{i})
, then
u_{i}:=w_{i}-e^{-\frac{\tau}{m}}\psi_{i}=U(\cdot;u_{i},\psi)
, and by Lemma
A.4
,

\begin{split}\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}&\lesssim_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}(\|u_{1}\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}+\|u_{2}\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}+\|\psi_{1}\|+\|\psi_{2}\|)(\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}+\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|)\\
&\lesssim_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}\varkappa(\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}+\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|)\,.\end{split}

Since
\ell,\eta
are fixed with respect to
m,\sigma
, by choosing
\varkappa(m,\sigma)>0
sufficiently small,
\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}
on the right-hand side can be absorbed by the left-hand side, and hence

\|u_{1}-u_{2}\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}\lesssim_{m,\sigma}\varkappa\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|\,.

Recalling the definition of
\|\cdot\|_{\ell,\sigma,\eta}
in (
A.3
), combine this with Sobolev embedding and notice that
u_{i}=w_{i}-e^{-\frac{\tau}{m}}\psi_{i}
, we get

\|(w_{1}-w_{2})(\cdot,\tau)-e^{-\frac{\tau}{m}}(\psi_{1}-\psi_{2})\|_{C^{4}(\mathbb{S}^{m})}\lesssim_{m,\sigma}\varkappa e^{-\sigma\tau}\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{m})}\,.

The discussion above yields the same estimate if
\varkappa
is replaced by any
\varkappa^{\prime}\in(0,\varkappa]
, and
\|\psi_{i}\|\leq\varkappa^{\prime}
.
∎

Remark A.5
.

Suppose
\mathbf{B}\in\mathrm{O}(m+1)
is an element in the rotation group of the sphere, and
\psi\in B_{r}^{\mathfrak{X}_{m}}
. Note that the expression of rescaled mean curvature flow and the linearized operator is invariant under rotation. Therefore, the expression of
\mathscr{N}
is also invariant under rotation, i.e.,
\mathscr{N}(f\circ\mathbf{B})=\mathscr{N}(f)\circ\mathbf{B}
. This implies that
U(\tau;u\circ\mathbf{B},\phi\circ\mathbf{B})=U(\tau;u,\phi)\circ\mathbf{B}
. As a consequence,
\mathfrak{T}(u\circ\mathbf{B},\phi\circ\mathbf{B})=\mathfrak{T}(u,\phi)\circ\mathbf{B}
, which implies that
\Xi(\psi\circ\mathbf{B})=\Xi(\psi)\circ\mathbf{B}
. In particular, by the uniqueness in the fixed point theorem, this implies that if
\psi\circ\mathbf{B}=\psi
, then
\Xi(\psi)\circ\mathbf{B}=\Xi(\psi)
.

A.3.
Proof of Lemma
3.4
.

We first construct a family of spherical arrival time functions
\mathscr{V}
in
\mathbb{R}^{n-k+1}
, and then define

\mathscr{U}:=\{\mathbf{u}:\mathbf{u}(x,y)=\mathbf{u}_{\circ}(x),\,\mathbf{u}_{\circ}\in\mathscr{V}\}.

As a family of rescaled mean curvature flows,
\mathscr{V}
is explicitly constructed in Appendix
A
as the image of the closed ball of radius
\kappa_{\ref{Lem_L^2Noncon_LowSphericalMode}}(n)\leq\varkappa
in Lemma
A.1
with
m=n-k
and
\sigma=3/(2m)=\frac{3}{2}\gamma_{n,k}
. In fact, any graphical function constructed in Appendix
A
, denoted by
\varphi_{\mathbf{u}_{\circ}}
, is the rescaled mean curvature flow for some spherical arrival time function
\mathbf{u}_{\circ}
in
\mathbb{R}^{n-k+1}
. We check that when
\kappa_{\ref{Lem_L^2Noncon_LowSphericalMode}}(n)
is chosen sufficiently small,
\mathscr{U}
satisfies the properties of this lemma.

To prove item
(1)
, because we extend
\mathbf{u}
based on the spherical part, we only need to consider those
\mathbf{B}
that rotate the spherical part of
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
. Then the proof of item
(1)
is a consequence of Remark
A.5
.

To prove item
(2)
, we only need to check that there exists
\beta(n)>0
so that

(
T2
)

holds for all
\mathbf{u}_{\circ}\in\mathscr{V}
. Let us estimate
-\nabla^{2}\mathbf{u}_{\circ}
. In
[
Hui93
, Page 189]
, Huisken showed that at the singular point,
D^{2}\mathbf{u}_{\circ}=-I/(n-k)
; at any regular point, for any unit vector
\nu
,

D_{\nu}D_{\nu}\mathbf{u}_{\circ}=-\frac{1}{(n-k)}-\left(\frac{\Delta H}{H^{3}}+\frac{|A|^{2}-H^{2}/(n-k)}{H^{2}}\right).

Note that the above quantity is exactly
-1/(n-k)
for the shrinking sphere, and in general,
D^{2}\mathbf{u}_{\circ}+I/(n-k)
is uniformly bounded by the
C^{4}
-norm of
\varphi_{\mathbf{u}_{\circ}}
, which is small whenever
\kappa_{\ref{Lem_L^2Noncon_LowSphericalMode}}
small, in terms of item
(3)
. This gives the uniform Hessian bound for
\mathbf{u}_{\circ}
.

To prove item
(3)
, because
\varphi_{\mathbf{u}}(\theta,y)=\varphi_{\mathbf{u}_{\circ}}(\theta)
, we only need to prove the desired estimates for
\mathbf{u}_{\circ}
. The estimates are direct results of items
(i)
and
(iii)
of Lemma
A.1
by the choice of
\sigma
above, and possibly choosing
\kappa_{\ref{Lem_L^2Noncon_LowSphericalMode}}(n)
smaller.

Appendix B
Analysis of parabolic Jacobi equation

Proposition B.1
.

Suppose
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\mathbb{B}_{\varepsilon}(\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}))
,
\lambda^{\prime}\geq\lambda+1+\varepsilon
,
|\lambda|,|\lambda^{\prime}|\leq\varepsilon^{-1}
. Suppose
h\in C^{\alpha}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0})
satisfies the pointwise bound,

|h(\theta,y,\tau)|\leq e^{-\lambda\tau}(1+|y|^{2})^{\lambda^{\prime}}\,.

Then there exists a solution
w\in C^{2,\alpha}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0})
to

\displaystyle\begin{cases}(\partial_{\tau}-L_{n,k})w=h\,,&\text{ on }\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\times\mathbb{R}_{>0}\,,\\
\Pi_{\geq\lambda}w(\cdot,0)=0\,,&\text{ on }\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\,.\end{cases}

with pointwise estimate,

|w(\theta,y,\tau)|\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-\lambda\tau}(1+|y|^{2})^{\lambda^{\prime}}\,.

Proposition B.2
.

Suppose
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,
\varepsilon^{\prime}\in(0,\varepsilon]
;
\lambda,\lambda^{\prime},\mu>0
satisfy

\displaystyle[\mu-\varepsilon^{\prime},\mu+\varepsilon^{\prime}]\cap\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})\subset\{\mu\}\,,

\displaystyle\mu+\varepsilon\leq\lambda\leq\min(\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})\cap\mathbb{R}_{>\mu})-\varepsilon\,,

\displaystyle|\lambda|,|\lambda^{\prime}|\leq\varepsilon^{-1}\,,

\displaystyle\lambda^{\prime}\geq\lambda+1+\varepsilon\,.

Let
w\in C^{2}_{loc}\cap L^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\times\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0})
be a solution to

\displaystyle(\partial_{\tau}-L_{n,k})w=0\,,

on
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\times\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, satisfying

\displaystyle\limsup_{\tau\to+\infty}e^{(\mu-\varepsilon^{\prime})\tau}\|w(\cdot,\tau)\|_{L^{2}}<+\infty\,,

\displaystyle\liminf_{\tau\to+\infty}e^{(\mu+\varepsilon^{\prime})\tau}\|w(\cdot,\tau)\|_{L^{2}}>0\,,

and pointwise bound on initial data,

|w(\theta,y,0)|\leq(1+|y|^{2})^{\lambda^{\prime}}\,.

Then
\mu\in\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})
,
\psi:=\Pi_{=\mu}(w(\cdot,0))
is non-zero, and the following pointwise estimate holds:

\displaystyle|w(\theta,y,\tau)-e^{-\mu\tau}\psi(\theta,y)|\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-\lambda\tau}(1+|y|^{2})^{\lambda^{\prime}}\,.

In the following, we fix
n,k
, and let us denote
e^{\tau L}
as the heat semi-group for
L_{n,k}
. The proof relies on the following estimates of the parabolic Jacobi fields. First, we prove a crude bound on the parabolic Jacobi fields.

Lemma B.3
.

Suppose
\lambda^{\prime}\geq\lambda+1+\varepsilon
,
|\lambda|,|\lambda^{\prime}|\leq\varepsilon^{-1}
;
|h(\theta,y)|\leq(1+|y|^{2})^{\lambda^{\prime}}
and
\Pi_{<\lambda}h=0
, then

(B.1)

|e^{\tau L}h(\theta,y)|\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-\lambda\tau}(1+|y|^{2})^{\lambda^{\prime}}.

Proof.

Recall that
L=L_{n,k}=\Delta_{\mathcal{C}_{n,k}}-\frac{y}{2}\cdot\nabla_{y}+1
. We first observe that,

\displaystyle(\partial_{\tau}-L)\left(e^{-\lambda\tau}(1+|y|^{2})^{\lambda^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=e^{-\lambda\tau}(1+|y|^{2})^{\lambda^{\prime}-1}\left((-\lambda-1-2k\lambda^{\prime})+(\lambda^{\prime}-\lambda-1)|y|^{2}-\frac{2\lambda^{\prime}(2\lambda^{\prime}-2)|y|^{2}}{1+|y|^{2}}\right)\,.

This implies that
\exists\,R=R(n,\varepsilon)>0
such that when
|y|\geq R
,
(\partial_{\tau}-L)(e^{-\lambda\tau}(1+|y|^{2})^{\lambda^{\prime}})>0
.

Now we will estimate
|e^{\tau L}h(\theta,y)|
for
|y|\leq R
and
|y|>R
separately. First, by the Fourier decomposition and
\Pi_{<\lambda}h=0
,

\|e^{\tau L}h\|_{L^{2}}\leq e^{-\lambda\tau}\|h\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-\lambda\tau}\,.

Then by the local weak maximum principle of parabolic PDE (see e.g.
[
Lie96
, Chapter VII Section 9]
), we have

(B.2)

\sup_{|y|\leq R}|e^{\tau L}h(\theta,y)|\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}\|e^{\tau L}h\|_{L^{2}_{c}(B_{2R})}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}\|e^{\tau L}h\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-\lambda\tau}\,.

Here
\|\cdot\|_{L^{2}_{c}}
is the classical un-weighted
L^{2}
norm. We used the fact that
R=R(n,\varepsilon)
.

Next we estimate
|e^{\tau L}h(\theta,y)|
for
|y|>R
. We need the following maximum principle on the unbounded region. Recall that
Q_{R}=B^{n-k+1}_{R}\times B_{R}^{k}
.

Lemma B.4
.

Suppose
R>2n
,
w(\cdot,\tau)\in L^{2}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\backslash Q_{R})
satisfies

\begin{cases}(\partial_{\tau}-L)w(\theta,y,\tau)\leq 0,&\text{on $(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\backslash Q_{R})\times\mathbb{R}_{>0}$},\\
w(\theta,y,\tau)\leq 0,&\text{on $\partial(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\backslash Q_{R})\times\mathbb{R}_{>0}$},\\
w(\theta,y,0)\leq 0,&\text{on $\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\backslash Q_{R}$}.\end{cases}

Then
w(\theta,y,\tau)\leq 0
on
(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\backslash Q_{R})\times\mathbb{R}_{>0}
.

Proof.

Let
w_{+}=\max\{0,w\}
and
d\mu:=e^{-|y|^{2}/4}dyd\theta
. Then

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\backslash Q_{R}}(\partial_{\tau}w-Lw)w_{+}d\mu

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\tau}\int_{\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\backslash Q_{R}}w_{+}^{2}+\int_{\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\backslash Q_{R}}|\nabla w_{+}|^{2}d\mu-\int_{\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\backslash Q_{R}}ww_{+}d\mu+\int_{\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap\partial B_{R}}\partial_{\nu}w\,w_{+}d\mu

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\tau}\int_{\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\backslash B_{R}}w_{+}^{2}-\int_{\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\backslash B_{R}}w_{+}^{2}d\mu\,.

where
\nu
denotes the outward unit normal of
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{R}
in
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
, and note that
\nabla w_{+}=0
a.e. on
\{w_{+}=0\}
.
Here in the last inequality, we use the fact that
w_{+}=0
on
\partial Q_{R}
. Therefore we have
\partial_{\tau}[e^{-2\tau}\int_{\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\backslash Q_{R}}w_{+}^{2}d\mu]\leq 0
, which implies that
w(\theta,y,\tau)\leq 0
on
(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\backslash Q_{R})\times\mathbb{R}_{>0}
.
∎

Now we apply this lemma to
\pm e^{\tau L}h(\theta,y)-\bar{C}e^{-\lambda\tau}(1+|y|^{2})^{\lambda^{\prime}}
, where here
\bar{C}=\bar{C}(n,\varepsilon)>1
is chosen large enough such that
|e^{\tau L}h(\theta,y)|\leq\bar{C}e^{-\lambda\tau}(1+|R|^{2})^{\lambda^{\prime}}
for
|y|=R
, according to (
B.2
). Then we obtain that
|e^{\tau L}h(\theta,y)|\leq\bar{C}(n,\varepsilon)e^{-\lambda\tau}(1+|y|^{2})^{\lambda^{\prime}}
when
|y|>R
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
B.1
.

The existence of the solution can be easily seen from the Fourier decomposition. In fact, suppose
h(\cdot,\tau)=\sum_{j\geq 0}a_{j}(\tau)\phi_{j}
is the
L^{2}
-decomposition, then we can construct

(B.3)

w(\cdot,\tau)=\sum_{\lambda_{j}<\lambda}\phi_{j}e^{-\lambda_{j}\tau}\left(-\int_{\tau}^{\infty}a_{j}(s)e^{\lambda_{j}s}ds\right)+\sum_{\lambda_{j}>\lambda}\phi_{j}e^{-\lambda_{j}\tau}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau}a_{j}(s)e^{\lambda_{j}s}ds\right)\,.

We first show that this solution is well-defined. The pointwise bound of
|h(\cdot,\tau)|
implies that weighted-
L^{2}
norm of
h(\cdot,\tau)
is also bounded by
e^{-\lambda\tau}
, therefore
|a_{j}(\tau)|\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-\lambda\tau}
, and hence

\left|\int_{\tau}^{\tau^{\prime}}a_{j}(s)e^{\lambda_{j}s}\ ds\right|\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}\left|e^{(\lambda_{j}-\lambda)\tau}-e^{(\lambda_{j}-\lambda)\tau^{\prime}}\right|\,,

So
w
is well-defined. In particular,

\left|\Pi_{<\lambda}w(\cdot,\tau)\right|=\left|\sum_{\lambda_{j}<\lambda}\phi_{j}e^{-\lambda_{j}\tau}\left(-\int_{\tau}^{\infty}a_{j}(s)e^{\lambda_{j}s}\ ds\right)\right|\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-\lambda\tau}(1+|y|^{2})^{\lambda^{\prime}-\varepsilon}.

Here we use the fact that
|\phi_{j}(\theta,y)|\lesssim_{\varepsilon}(1+|y|^{2})^{\lambda_{j}+1}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}(1+|y|^{2})^{\lambda+1}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}(1+|y|^{2})^{\lambda^{\prime}-\varepsilon}
when
\lambda_{j}<\lambda
.

Next we estimate
\left|\Pi_{>\lambda}w(\cdot,\tau)\right|
. By Duhamel’s principle and the assumption of
\lambda
,

\Pi_{>\lambda}w(\cdot,\tau)=\Pi_{>\lambda+\varepsilon}w(\cdot,\tau)=\int_{0}^{\tau}e^{(\tau-s)L}\Pi_{>\lambda+\varepsilon}h(\cdot,s)\ ds.

Then we use Lemma
B.3
and the fact that
|\Pi_{>\lambda+\varepsilon}h(\cdot,s)|\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-\lambda s}(1+|y|^{2})^{\lambda^{\prime}}
to obtain,

\displaystyle\left|\Pi_{>\lambda}w(\cdot,\tau)\right|\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\tau}e^{-(\lambda+\varepsilon)(\tau-s)}\cdot e^{-\lambda s}(1+|y|^{2})^{\lambda^{\prime}}\ ds\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}e^{-\lambda\tau}(1+|y|^{2})^{\lambda^{\prime}}.

Combining the estimates of
\left|\Pi_{>\lambda}w(\cdot,\tau)\right|
and
\left|\Pi_{<\lambda}w(\cdot,\tau)\right|
gives the desired bound.
∎

Proof of Proposition
B.2
.

First of all, because
w
is an
L^{2}
-solution, we can write it down by the Fourier decomposition

(B.4)

w(\cdot,\tau)=\sum_{j\geq 0}a_{j}e^{-\lambda_{j}\tau}\phi_{j}.

Suppose
K
is the smallest integer such that
a_{K}\neq 0
. Then
\|w(\cdot,\tau)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}=\sum_{j\geq K}|a_{j}|^{2}e^{-2\lambda_{j}\tau}
. In particular, we have

e^{(\mu-\varepsilon^{\prime})\tau}\|w(\cdot,\tau)\|_{L^{2}}\geq e^{(\mu-\varepsilon^{\prime})\tau}|a_{K}|e^{-\lambda_{K}\tau},\qquad e^{(\mu+\varepsilon^{\prime})\tau}\|w(\cdot,\tau)\|_{L^{2}}\leq e^{(\mu+\varepsilon^{\prime})\tau}e^{-\lambda_{K}\tau}\|w(\cdot,0)\|_{L^{2}}.

This implies that
\lambda_{K}\in[\mu-\varepsilon^{\prime},\mu+\varepsilon^{\prime}]
, and hence,
\lambda_{K}=\mu
. This proves that
\mu\in\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})
.

Suppose
\psi:=\Pi_{=\mu}(w(\cdot,0))
. Because
\mu+\varepsilon\leq\lambda\leq\lambda^{\prime}-1-\varepsilon<\varepsilon^{-1}
, we have
|\psi(\theta,y)|\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}(1+|y|^{2})^{\lambda^{\prime}}
. This implies that
|w(\theta,y,0)-\psi(\theta,y)|\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon}(1+|y|^{2})^{\lambda^{\prime}}
. So we can apply Lemma
B.3
to get the desired bound.
∎

We also recall some basic properties of parabolic Jacobi fields over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
. The proof can be found in
[
SWX25
, Appendix A]
.

Lemma B.5
.

Let
a+1<b
,
v\in C^{2}_{loc}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\times(a,b))
be a non-zero function so that
\|v(\cdot,\tau)\|_{L^{2}}<+\infty
for every
\tau\in(a,b)
and it solves

\displaystyle(\partial_{\tau}-L_{n,k})v=0

on
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\times(a,b)
. Define the
linear decay order
of
v
at time
\tau
by

N_{n,k}(\tau;v):=\log\left(\frac{\|v(\cdot,\tau)\|_{L^{2}}}{\|v(\cdot,\tau+1)\|_{L^{2}}}\right).

Then we have,

(i)

N_{n,k}(\tau;v)\geq-1
and is monotone non-increasing in
\tau\in(a,b-1)
.

(ii)

If for some
\tau_{0}\in(a,b)
,
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
and
\sim\in\{\geq,>,=,<,\leq\}
, we have
\|\Pi_{\sim\gamma}(v(\cdot,\tau_{0}))\|_{L^{2}}=\|v(\cdot,\tau_{0})\|_{L^{2}}
, where
\Pi_{\sim\gamma}
is defined in (
2.7
). Then for every
\tau\in(a,b-1)
,

N_{n,k}(\tau;v)\sim\gamma\,.

(iii)

If for some
\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}\in(a,b-1)
,
N_{n,k}(\tau_{1};v)=N_{n,k}(\tau_{2};v)=\gamma
, then

v(X,\tau)=e^{-\gamma\tau}\psi(X)

for some non-zero eigenfunction
\psi\in\mathrm{W}_{\gamma}(\mathcal{C}_{n,k})
of
-L_{n,k}
.

Appendix C
Graphs over generalized cylinders

Throughout this section, we parametrize
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}=\mathbb{S}^{n-k}\times\mathbb{R}^{k}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
by
(\theta,y)
as before. Note that for every
(\theta,y)\in\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
,
|\theta|^{2}=2(n-k)=:\varrho^{2}
. Let
\hat{\theta}:=\theta/|\theta|
.

Let
\Omega=\mathbb{S}^{n-k}\times\Omega_{\circ}\subset\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
be a subdomain,
v\in C^{1}(\Omega)
such that
\inf v>-\sqrt{2(n-k)}
. We use
\nabla_{\theta}v
and
\nabla_{y}v
to denote the components of
\nabla v
parallel to
\mathbb{S}^{n-k}
factor and
\mathbb{R}^{k}
factor correspondingly. For later reference, we also denote

\displaystyle\hat{\nabla}_{\theta}v:=\left(1+\frac{v}{|\theta|}\right)^{-1}\cdot\nabla_{\theta}v\,,

\displaystyle\hat{\nabla}v:=\hat{\nabla}_{\theta}v+\nabla_{y}v\,.

In the following,
\mathbf{u}\in\mathscr{U}
is an arrival time function that we constructed in Lemma
3.4
.

Lemma C.1
.

Let
\Omega,v
be specified as above, let

\Sigma=\mathrm{graph}_{\mathcal{C}_{n,k}}(v)=\{(\theta+v(\theta,y)\hat{\theta},y):(\theta,y)\in\Omega\}\,.

And we parametrized
\Sigma
by

\Phi_{v}:\Omega\to\Sigma\,,\quad(\theta,y)\mapsto(\theta+v(\theta,y)\hat{\theta},y)\,.

Then
\Sigma
is a hypersurface in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
,
\Phi_{v}
is a diffeomorphism and we have the following for
\tau\geq 0
.

(i)

For every
(\theta,y)\in\Omega
,

\overline{\dist}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\Phi_{v}(\theta,y))=\chi(v(\theta,y)-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}}(\theta,\tau)),

(ii)

The unit normal field of
\Sigma
pointing away from
\{\mathbf{0}\}\times\mathbb{R}^{k}
is

\nu_{\Sigma}|_{\Phi_{v}(\theta,y)}=\left.(1+|\hat{\nabla}v|^{2})^{-1/2}\cdot\left(\hat{\theta}-\hat{\nabla}v\right)\right|_{(\theta,y)}\,,

(iii)

For every function
f\in C^{0}(\Sigma)
,

\int_{\Sigma}f(x)\ dx=\int_{\Omega}f\circ\Phi_{v}(X)\mathcal{A}[v]\ dX\,,

where

\mathcal{A}[v](\theta,y):=\left(1+\frac{v}{|\theta|}\right)^{n-k}\cdot\sqrt{1+|\hat{\nabla}v|^{2}}\,.

In particular, there exists
\kappa_{\ref{Lem_App_Graph over Cylinder}}(n)\in(0,1/2)
such that if
\|\varphi_{\mathbf{u}}(\cdot,\tau)\|_{C^{1}},\,\|v\|_{C^{1}}\leq\kappa_{\ref{Lem_App_Graph over Cylinder}}(n)
, then

\left|\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\Sigma)^{-1}\cdot\|v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}}(\cdot,\tau)\|_{L^{2}}-1\right|\lesssim_{n}\|v\|_{C^{1}}\,.

(iv)

\exists\,\kappa^{\prime}_{\ref{Lem_App_Graph over Cylinder}}(n)\in(0,1/2)
such that if
(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\in\mathbb{R}^{n-k+1}\times\mathbb{R}^{k}
,
\mathbf{A}\in\mathfrak{g}_{n,k}^{\perp}
,
\lambda>0
,
R>2n
satisfy,

\delta:=\|v\|_{C^{2}(Q_{R}(0,\lambda^{-1}\mathbf{y}))}+|\mathbf{x}|+R|\lambda-1|+R|\mathbf{A}|\leq\kappa^{\prime}_{\ref{Lem_App_Graph over Cylinder}}\,,

then in
Q_{R-C_{n}\delta}
,
e^{\mathbf{A}}(\lambda\Sigma-(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))
is also a graph over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
, and the graphical function
\bar{v}
satisfies,

(C.1)

\begin{split}\sup_{(\theta^{\prime},y^{\prime})\in\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{R-C_{n}\delta}}&\,\left|\bar{v}(\theta^{\prime},y^{\prime})-v(\theta^{\prime},\lambda^{-1}\mathbf{y}+y^{\prime})-\varrho(\lambda-1)+\psi_{\mathbf{x}}(\theta^{\prime})-\psi_{\mathbf{A}}(\theta^{\prime},y^{\prime})\right|\\
&\lesssim_{n}\delta(|\mathbf{x}|+R|\lambda-1|+R|\mathbf{A}|)\,.\end{split}

where
\psi_{\mathbf{x}},\psi_{\mathbf{A}}
are eigenfunctions of
-L_{n,k}
defined in (
2.10
) and (
2.11
) in the Preliminary.

Proof.

The items
(i)
,
(ii)
and
(iii)
are routine. In
(iv)
, the fact the
e^{\mathbf{A}}(\lambda\Sigma-(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))
is a graph over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
in
Q_{R-C_{n}\delta}
follows from implicit function theorem. So we only focus on the proof of (
C.1
).

Given a point
(\theta+v(\theta,y)\hat{\theta},y)
in the graph over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\cap Q_{R}(0,\lambda^{-1}\mathbf{y})
, we suppose

X^{\prime}:=\left(\theta^{\prime}+\bar{v}(\theta^{\prime},y^{\prime})\hat{\theta}^{\prime},\,y^{\prime}\right)=e^{\mathbf{A}}\left(\lambda\left(\theta+v(\theta,y)\hat{\theta},\,y\right)-(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\right)

be the point after the transformation. Note that
\mathbf{A}\in\mathfrak{g}_{n,k}^{\perp}
, we may assume
\mathbf{A}=\begin{bmatrix}0&\mathbf{\ell}\\
-\mathbf{\ell}^{\top}&0\end{bmatrix}
. Also note that we have an a priori bound
|X^{\prime}|\lesssim_{n}R
. Then we can write

(C.2)

\displaystyle\theta^{\prime}+\bar{v}(\theta^{\prime},y^{\prime})\hat{\theta}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\lambda\left(\theta+v(\theta,y)\hat{\theta}\right)-\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{\ell}(\lambda y-\mathbf{y})+O(|\mathbf{A}|^{2}R)\,,

(C.3)

\displaystyle y^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\lambda y-\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{\ell}^{\top}\left(\lambda\left(\theta+v(\theta,y)\hat{\theta}\right)-\mathbf{x}\right)+O(|\mathbf{A}|^{2}R)\,.

Also, by the expression
e^{-\mathbf{A}}(\theta^{\prime}+\bar{v}(\theta^{\prime},y^{\prime})\hat{\theta}^{\prime},\,y^{\prime})=\lambda(\theta+v(\theta,y)\hat{\theta},\,y)-(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})
, we can write

(C.4)

\displaystyle\lambda y-\mathbf{y}=y^{\prime}+\mathbf{\ell}^{\top}(\theta^{\prime}+\bar{v}(\theta^{\prime},y^{\prime})\hat{\theta}^{\prime})+O(|\mathbf{A}|^{2}R)\,.

If we take the inner product of (
C.2
) with
\hat{\theta}^{\prime}
, by (
2.10
) and (
2.11
) we get

\varrho+\bar{v}(\theta^{\prime},y^{\prime})=\lambda\varrho+\lambda v(\theta,y)\hat{\theta}\cdot\hat{\theta}^{\prime}-\psi_{\mathbf{x}}(\theta^{\prime})+\psi_{\mathbf{A}}(\theta^{\prime},\lambda y-\mathbf{y})+O(|\mathbf{A}|^{2}R)\,,

which implies that
|\bar{v}(\theta^{\prime},y^{\prime})|\lesssim_{n}|\lambda-1|+|v(\theta,y)|+|\mathbf{x}|+R|\mathbf{A}|\lesssim_{n}\delta
and,

(C.5)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\bar{v}(\theta^{\prime},y^{\prime})-v(\theta^{\prime},\lambda^{-1}\mathbf{y}+y^{\prime})-\varrho(\lambda-1)+\psi_{\mathbf{x}}(\theta^{\prime})-\psi_{\mathbf{A}}(\theta^{\prime},y^{\prime})\\
&\quad=\left(\lambda v(\theta,y)\hat{\theta}\cdot\hat{\theta}^{\prime}-v(\theta^{\prime},\lambda^{-1}\mathbf{y}+y^{\prime})\right)+\psi_{\mathbf{A}}(\theta^{\prime},\lambda y-\mathbf{y}-y^{\prime})+O(|\mathbf{A}|^{2}R)\,.\end{split}

Now it suffices to estimate the terms on the right-hand side. If we project (
C.2
) onto
\mathbb{S}^{n-k}
and take
\kappa^{\prime}_{\ref{Lem_App_Graph over Cylinder}}(n)\ll 1
, we get

|\hat{\theta}^{\prime}-\hat{\theta}|\lesssim_{n}\left|-\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{\ell}(\lambda y-\mathbf{y})+O(|\mathbf{A}|^{2}R)\right|\lesssim_{n}|\mathbf{x}|+|\mathbf{A}|R

And hence

|1-\hat{\theta}\cdot\hat{\theta}^{\prime}|=\frac{1}{2}|\hat{\theta}^{\prime}-\hat{\theta}|^{2}\lesssim_{n}|\mathbf{x}|+|\mathbf{A}|R\,.

Combining these with (
C.4
) yields,

\displaystyle\left|\lambda v(\theta,y)\hat{\theta}\cdot\hat{\theta}^{\prime}-v(\theta^{\prime},\lambda^{-1}\mathbf{y}+y^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle\leq(|\lambda-1|+|\mathbf{x}|+|\mathbf{A}|R)|v(\theta,y)|+|v(\theta,y)-v(\theta^{\prime},\lambda^{-1}\mathbf{y}+y^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n}(R|\lambda-1|+|\mathbf{x}|+R|\mathbf{A}|)\cdot\|v\|_{C^{1}(Q_{R}(0,\lambda^{-1}\mathbf{y}))}\,;

and

|\psi_{\mathbf{A}}(\theta^{\prime},\lambda y-\mathbf{y}-y^{\prime})|\lesssim_{n}|\mathbf{A}||\lambda y-\mathbf{y}-y^{\prime}|\lesssim_{n}|\mathbf{A}|^{2}\,.

Together with (
C.5
), these proves (
C.1
).
∎

The item
(iii)
of Lemma
C.1
shows that
\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\tau,\Sigma)
is comparable to
\|v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}}(\cdot,\tau)\|_{L^{2}}
, if
\Sigma
is a graph of function
v
over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
. Therefore, by the classical Schauder theory, we can use
\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(\Sigma)
to bound the
C^{2}
-norm of
v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}}
. We first recall the equation of the graph function of a rescaled mean curvature flow over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
. Suppose
v(\cdot,\tau)
is the graph function of a rescaled mean curvature flow over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
, then it satisfies the equation

(C.6)

\partial_{\tau}v=L_{n,k}v+\mathcal{Q}(v,\nabla v,\nabla^{2}v)=:L_{n,k}v+\mathcal{Q}[v],

where
\mathcal{Q}
is a smooth function in
(z,\xi,\eta)\in\mathbb{R}\times T\mathcal{C}_{n,k}\times T^{\otimes 2}\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
with

\mathcal{Q}(0,0,0)=\mathcal{Q}_{z}(0,0,0)=\mathcal{Q}_{\xi}(0,0,0)=\mathcal{Q}_{\eta}(0,0,0)=0\,.

The precise calculations in various formulations can be found in Appendix A of
[
CM15
]
, Appendix A of
[
SX25b
]
, among others.

Lemma C.2
.

For every
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2)
and
R>2n
, there exists
\delta_{\ref{Lem_L^2 clos => C^2 close}}(\varepsilon,n,R)\in(0,\varepsilon)
such that the following hold. If
T\geq 0
,
\delta\in(0,\delta_{\ref{Lem_L^2 clos => C^2 close}}]
,
\mathbf{u}\in\mathscr{U}
satisfies
\|\varphi_{\mathbf{u}}(\cdot,\tau)\|\leq\delta
for
\tau\in[T,T+1]
(see the notations in Section
3.2
), and
\mathcal{M}
is a RMCF in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
over
[T,T+1]
such that

\lambda[\mathcal{M}]\leq\varepsilon^{-1}\,,\qquad\text{$\mathcal{M}$ is $\delta$-$L^{2}$ close to $\mathcal{C}_{n,k}$}\,.

Then
\mathcal{M}(T+1)
is a
C^{2}
graph over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
in
Q_{R}
with graphical function
v(\cdot,T+1)
satisfying

\|(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})(\cdot,T+1)\|_{C^{2}(Q_{R}\cap\mathcal{C}_{n,k})}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon,R}\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(T,\mathcal{M})\,.

Proof.

Let
\varepsilon_{1}\in(0,\delta_{0}(n))
to be determined. First by Brakke-White’s
\varepsilon
-regularity and a compactness argument, there exists
\delta^{\prime}(\varepsilon,n,R,\varepsilon_{1})\ll 1
such that if
\mathcal{M},\mathbf{u}
are specified as in the Lemma with
\delta\leq\delta^{\prime}(\varepsilon,n,R,\varepsilon_{1})
, then for all
\tau\in[1/2,1]
,
\mathcal{M}(T+\tau)
is a
C^{2}
graph over
\mathcal{C}_{n,k}
in
Q_{3R}
with graphical function
v(\cdot,T+\tau)
satisfies
\|v(\cdot,T+\tau)\|_{C^{4}(Q_{3R}\cap\mathcal{C}_{n,k})}\leq\varepsilon_{1}
, and
v
solves the RMCF equation (
C.6
).

Now we prove the
C^{2}
estimate of
v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}}
. Let
w(\cdot,\tau):=(v-\varphi_{\mathbf{u}})(\cdot,T+\tau)
, then inside
(Q_{3R}\cap\mathcal{C}_{n,k})\times[1/2,1]
,
w
satisfies the equation

\partial_{\tau}w=Lw+\mathcal{Q}[v]-\mathcal{Q}[\varphi_{\mathbf{u}}]=\partial_{\tau}w=Lw+aw+b_{j}\partial_{j}w+c_{ij}\partial_{ij}w,

where
a,b_{j},c_{ij}
are terms of the partial derivatives of
\mathcal{Q}
with variables involving
v
,
\nabla v
,
\nabla^{2}v
,
\varphi_{\mathbf{u}}
,
\nabla\varphi_{\mathbf{u}}
,
\nabla^{2}\varphi_{\mathbf{u}}
. Therefore, if we choose
\varepsilon_{1}=\varepsilon_{1}(n,R)
to be sufficiently small and
\delta_{\ref{Lem_L^2 clos => C^2 close}}=\delta^{\prime}(\varepsilon,n,R,\varepsilon_{1})
, we can make
|a|
,
|b_{j}|
,
|c_{ij}|
sufficiently small, such that
\partial_{\tau}w=Lw+aw+b_{j}\partial_{j}w+c_{ij}\partial_{ij}w
is a strictly parabolic equation with uniformly bounded coefficients in
(Q_{3R}\cap\mathcal{C}_{n,k})\times[1/2,1]
. Then the standard interior estimate of parabolic equation, e.g.
[
Lie96
, Lemma 7.38]
, shows that

\|w\|_{C^{0}((Q_{2R}\cap\mathcal{C}_{n,k})\times[3/4,1])}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon,R}\sup_{\tau\in[1/2,1]}\|w\|_{L^{2}(Q_{3R}\cap\mathcal{C}_{n,k})}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon,R}\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(T,\mathcal{M})\,,

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
C.1

(iii)
and Corollary
3.3
.
Finally, the interior Schauder estimate shows that

\|w\|_{C^{2,\alpha}((Q_{R}\cap\mathcal{C}_{n,k})\times[7/8,1])}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon,R}\|w\|_{C^{0}((Q_{2R}\cap\mathcal{C}_{n,k})\times[3/4,1])}\lesssim_{n,\varepsilon,R}\mathbf{d}_{\mathbf{u}}(T,\mathcal{M}).

∎
